######## PR #37 (2026-10-03T16:39:46Z) PR C2: entry points use aitools-lib logging, installers report every failure, logging unit tests
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/37  +2349/-265 files=10
FILES: .github/workflows/check.yml, CLAUDE.md, plans/linux-install-hardening.md, reference/script-standards-detail.md, scripts/aitools, scripts/aitools-install.ps1, scripts/aitools-install.sh, scripts/aitools.ps1, tests/logging/test-logging.ps1, tests/logging/test-logging.sh

Second part of PR C (logging conformance, epic #21). Plan: `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C2 — verbatim edits". It holds the decisions, the error-handling audit, the exact diff for each batch and the new **logging audit plan**.

## Behaviour changes you will see

- **`aitools` subcommand errors use the standard format.** `aitools` / `aitools.ps1` now load aitools-lib as soon as `repoPath` is read, and no longer define their own logging (#28, decision C-F9). Errors such as an unknown argument print as `[ts] [aitools] [error] …` and go to `deploy.log`. Before, they printed as a bare `error: …` on stderr.
  - Only `--help`, `--version` and the missing-repo re-clone run before the lib loads.
  - Warnings found before it loads are logged right after.
  - If the lib is missing, `aitools` prints one stderr line naming the path and exits 1. `--version` still answers.
- **The end-of-run summary now has an ERROR row for every child script that fails, has a syntax error, or fails to parse.** This covers `aitools` sync and both installers. The log gives the real exit code; before, it always said "exit 0".
- **`aitools install` without a terminal and without gh auth now shows a WARN row plus "Run: gh auth login".** This follows the post-install auth-check rule.

## Changes

| Batch | Files | Issues |
|---|---|---|
| C3b | `aitools`, `aitools.ps1` | #22, #23, #25, #26e, #27b, #28 |
| C4b | `aitools-install.sh`, `aitools-install.ps1` | #23, #25, #26b (PS1 Step 5 backup and summary row), #26e, #27a (`claude update` decided by exit code); dead Windows branches removed from the bash installer |
| T1 | `tests/logging/test-logging.sh`, `tests/logging/test-logging.ps1` (new), `.github/workflows/check.yml` | Logging unit tests (lib contract and entry-point use of the lib), run on macOS, Linux and Windows CI |
| D-C2 | `reference/script-standards-detail.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, plan | `scripts/aitools` row removed from the logging-overrides table; `tests/` line; plan section and audit plan |

Release notes are deferred, at the commander's request. No `deploy/` rebuild is needed: no lib change, and `build-deploy.sh` doesn't embed the entry points or the installers.

## Verification (Linux)

|---|---|---|
| `tests/logging/test-logging.sh` (in repo) | 24/24 | 19/24 |
| `tests/logging/test-logging.ps1` (in repo) | 15/15, plus 5 Windows-only skips | — |

Each batch was applied by a fresh sub-agent from a patch and verified byte-identical to the prototype. The C4b batch audit found that two PS1 installer detail lines would print to the console; they now use `LogDetail`, and the plan diff is updated.

**Not tested here:** the PS1 entry point and installer top-level blocks run only on Windows (OS guard). The Windows cases in `test-logging.ps1` first run for real in this PR's CI. The bash paths still need a macOS run.

## Follow-ups (in the plan, not this PR)

- Move `aitools-install`'s JSONL output into the lib, so no script overrides logging. The aitools-lib header comment gets fixed then.
- Batch C15: turn the audit scanner into `check-pre-commit` steps (warnings first, failures once a count reaches zero).
- `git checkout HEAD -- deploy/ 2>/dev/null || true` before each pull.
- The `profile:` lines printed with raw `printf` / `Write-Host`.
- `Read-Host` for the machine alias in `aitools.ps1`.
- With the repo missing, a mistyped command re-clones the repo before it is rejected.

######## PR #38 (2026-10-03T17:00:46Z) PR C3 (batch C5): rust/typst setup log full output and decide on exit codes; pre-push step 7 checks the dotprofile deploy/
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/38  +751/-66 files=9
FILES: plans/linux-install-hardening.md, reference/script-standards-detail.md, scripts/check-pre-commit.ps1, scripts/check-pre-commit.sh, scripts/check-pre-push.ps1, scripts/check-pre-push.sh, scripts/setup-rust.ps1, scripts/setup-rust.sh, scripts/setup-typst.sh

Third part of PR C (logging conformance, epic #21). Plan: `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C3 — verbatim edits (batch C5)". It has the change table, the error-handling audit, the tests, the audit counts, two addenda and the exact diff.

## Changes

**`setup-rust.sh` / `setup-rust.ps1`**
- **`rustup update`:** the full output is logged as detail; before, only the last 3 lines were kept (#22). The exit code decides success; before, the script searched the output for "error" or "fatal" (#27a). The ERROR row names the exit code and the log path (#27b).
- **Blank-line abort (bash):** the piped `[ -n ] && log` loop is gone. A blank last output line aborted the script under `set -e`; this is reproduced on `main` (#24).
- **Homebrew rust (bash):** it's detected with `brew list --versions`. A failed uninstall logs its output and warns with the exit code (#23).
- **Version checks:** they no longer throw away stderr. The PS1 vswhere and nasm probes keep `2>$null`, now with a comment; both results are checked.

**`setup-typst.sh`**
- cargo `typst-cli` and npm `typst` are removed only when actually installed. Removal output is logged, and a failed removal warns with the exit code (#23). Before, both uninstalls ran unconditionally with output discarded.
- **`brew upgrade`:** a non-zero exit is now an ERROR even without "error" in the output (C-F2). The Standard 3 output check stays.
- The script is still Homebrew-only; Linux support is #14 (PR B).

**Pre-commit step 14:** `cargo install --list` only lists installed packages, so it no longer counts as a source build. It had flagged `setup-typst.sh`.

**Pre-push step 7 ("deploy/ matches source"):** it was stale. It required `deploy/` changes in the aitools push, but aitools' `deploy/` is frozen and generated scripts live in the dotprofile repo, so every setup-script change failed the step. It now:
- runs when setup scripts, `build-deploy.sh`, `shared/` or `aitools-lib.*` change (the lib was previously not counted);
- skips when no dotprofile is configured;
- otherwise rebuilds from this branch into a temporary copy of the dotprofile's committed tree and compares `deploy/`. It passes when they're identical and fails naming each stale file.

**Docs (D-C3):** the exemptions-table rows for `setup-rust.sh` and `setup-typst.sh` are removed, and the plan section is added. Release notes are deferred, at the commander's request.

Companion PR: nobul-jose/aitools-nobul-jose, which regenerates `deploy/setup-rust.sh`, `deploy/setup-rust.ps1` and `deploy/setup-typst.sh`.

## Verification (Linux)

| Suite | This branch | `main` |
|---|---|---|
| `setup-rust.sh` / `setup-typst.sh` with stubbed rustup/cargo/brew/npm/typst | 17/17 | 4/17 |
| `setup-rust.ps1`, OS guard stripped, stub `rustup.exe` | 4/4 | 2/4 |
| `tests/logging/test-logging.sh` | 24/24 | — |
| Pre-push step 7 (bash, and PS1 with OS guard stripped): rebuilt dotprofile / stale dotprofile / no dotprofile | PASS / FAIL naming the 3 files / SKIP | — |
| `build-deploy.sh` | 40 scripts; 3 changed, byte-identical to the prototype build; all parse | — |
| `check-pre-commit` / `check-pre-push` | 0 FAIL | — |

The C5 batch was applied by a fresh sub-agent from a patch and verified byte-identical to the prototype. Both check-script fixes were found by the pre-commit and pre-push gates during execution, and were approved by the commander before being changed.

**Not tested here:**
- The MSVC, NASM and persistent-PATH parts of `setup-rust.ps1` (Windows only; unchanged apart from comments).
- The Homebrew paths, which need a macOS run.
- The PS1 step 7 on real Windows.

## Known minor points (left as is)

- **`setup-typst.sh`:** `printf … | grep -q` under `pipefail` could misread a very large `cargo install --list` output. The output is small, and the `brew upgrade` checks use the same pattern.
- **`setup-rust.sh`:** if `rustup --version` ever prints no `rustup ` line, the OK log line is empty. Cosmetic only.

######## PR #39 (2026-10-03T17:08:17Z) PR C4 (batch C6): typst.ps1 and pandoc report cleanups and upgrades truthfully
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/39  +449/-46 files=5
FILES: plans/linux-install-hardening.md, reference/script-standards-detail.md, scripts/setup-pandoc.ps1, scripts/setup-pandoc.sh, scripts/setup-typst.ps1

This is the fourth part of PR C (logging conformance, epic #21). The plan is in `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C4 — verbatim edits (batch C6)". That section has the change table, the error-handling audit, the tests, the audit counts and the exact diff.

## Changes

**`setup-pandoc.sh`**
- **Missing Homebrew:** an ERROR summary row is now written before `exit 1`. Before, there was no row (#26a).
- **`brew upgrade`:** the exit code is captured. A non-zero exit is an ERROR even when the output doesn't say "error" (C-F2). The output is logged, and the ERROR row gives the exit code and the log path. The Standard 3 output grep stays.
- **Migration cleanups:** conda and MacPorts pandoc are removed only when they're listed. A conda `pandoc-crossref` on its own no longer triggers a removal. If a probe fails, the script warns. Removal output is logged, and a failed removal warns with its exit code. Before, all of this used `2>/dev/null || true` (#23).

**`setup-typst.ps1`**
- **Cargo and npm typst:** cargo `typst-cli` and npm `typst` are removed only when installed. The output is logged, and a failure warns with its exit code. Before, both were removed unconditionally with `2>$null | Out-Null` (#23). This matches what `setup-typst.sh` got in C5.
- **winget:** exit codes are saved before logging. After install, an empty `typst --version` result is now an ERROR.

**`setup-pandoc.ps1`**
- **`winget upgrade`:** the output is now logged. Before, it was captured and dropped (#23).
- **Exit codes:** they are saved before logging.
- **choco probe:** its exit code is checked, and its match is narrowed to a `pandoc ` line.

**Docs (D-C4):** the exemptions-table rows for `setup-pandoc.sh` and `setup-typst.ps1` are removed, and the plan section is added. Release notes are deferred at the commander's request.

Companion PR in nobul-jose/aitools-nobul-jose: it regenerates `deploy/setup-pandoc.sh`, `deploy/setup-pandoc.ps1` and `deploy/setup-typst.ps1`.

A fresh sub-agent applied the batch from a patch, and the result was verified byte-identical to the prototype.

######## PR #40 (2026-10-03T18:41:57Z) Logging conformance PR C5: setup-modal, setup-go.ps1, setup-rust (batches C7, C7b)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/40  +742/-102 files=5
FILES: plans/linux-install-hardening.md, scripts/setup-go.ps1, scripts/setup-modal.ps1, scripts/setup-modal.sh, scripts/setup-rust.sh

Part of epic #21 (logging conformance). Verbatim edits, error-handling audit and test evidence: `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C5 — verbatim edits (batches C7, C7b)".

## Batch C7 — #23, #26d, #27a
- **setup-modal.sh / .ps1**:
  - `uv tool upgrade` / `uv tool install` / `pip install` are decided by exit code. Before, an output grep decided: uv's exit-0 "Failed to hardlink" warning and pip's exit-0 resolver notice came out as ERRORs, and an exit-137 kill came out as OK.
  - The upgrade output is logged before a migration or env repair (before, it was overwritten or dropped). Command output goes to the log as detail lines.
  - ERROR rows name the exit code and the log path.
  - Upgrade exit 0 with modal gone from PATH is now an ERROR row (before: no row, exit 0).
  - An unreadable Python version on the pip path is an ERROR row (before: a silent `set -e` exit in bash, a skipped gate in PS1).
  - PS1 results are if/elseif/else chains, so each outcome gets exactly one row.
- **setup-go.ps1**:
  - `choco uninstall golang` output is logged.
  - winget exit codes are saved before logging and named in ERROR rows.
  - A failed winget step is no longer followed by an OK row or a second ERROR row.

**Windows note (for the release notes):** `setup-modal.ps1` still requires Python 3.10+ on PATH even when uv is present; macOS/Linux check it only for the pip fallback. Aligning it is pending validation on Windows. With uv present, an unreadable Python version is now logged and the install continues.

## Batch C7b
`setup-rust.sh` drops a `rustup --version | grep` pipeline added in PR C3 (#38). It tripped `check-script-compliance` step 7, which only runs at post-push. The output is unchanged. The plan's per-batch verification now includes `check-script-compliance.sh`.

## Plan
Adds the PR C5 section and updates the batch tables. Adds scoped batch V1: `--verbose` / `-Verbose` (and `AITOOLS_VERBOSE=1`) to print detail lines on the console as well.

## Testing (Linux)
- Stub-driven `setup-modal.sh` + C7b suite: 20/20 (`main`: 8/20).
- PS1 simulation of `setup-modal.ps1` / `setup-go.ps1`: 22/22 (`main`: 6/22).
- `check-script-compliance.sh`: 13 PASS, 0 WARN (`main`: 1 WARN).
- Pre-commit: 0 FAIL. Pre-push: 0 FAIL (step 7: dotprofile `deploy/` matches a fresh build).
- Logging audit: A3 190→187, A4 316→302, A8 9→7. No count rises.
- Not tested: real uv / pip / winget / Chocolatey runs on macOS and Windows.

Companion dotprofile PR: regenerated `deploy/` in nobul-jose/aitools-nobul-jose.

######## PR #42 (2026-10-04T20:41:43Z) Logging conformance PR C7: setup-user-cursor (batch C9)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/42  +491/-40 files=3

Part of epic #21 (logging conformance). Verbatim edits, error-handling audit and test evidence are in `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C7 — verbatim edits (batch C9)".

## Batch C9 (#23, #25, #26b–d)

**setup-user-cursor.sh**
- `brew install ripgrep` and the Cursor CLI installer (`curl | bash`) now log their output, and their exit code decides the result. Before, they were fire-and-forget, and any failure aborted the whole script under `set -e`.
- Install outcomes now write summary rows under `cursor cli`: a failure is ERROR; Homebrew missing or the tool not on PATH is WARN. Before, outcomes went into `STATUS_*` variables that nothing read.
- The node merge prints its diagnostics on stdout as `MSG:` / `WARN:` / `DETAIL:` lines, and bash logs them. Before, these were `console.error` lines that only reached the console: the corrupt-file warning, clobber list, dry-run preview and validation failure.
- A non-zero node exit writes an ERROR row. Before, it was a silent `set -e` abort with no row.

**setup-user-cursor.ps1**
- The winget ripgrep install is logged and its exit code checked.
- The Cursor installer runs in try/catch, with its output logged.
- The cli-config write runs in try/catch. A failed write or post-write validation writes an ERROR row and no OK row after it.

**build-deploy.sh is unchanged on purpose.** Every edit sits inside the extracted "cursor body" and outside the "profile preferences" block that the build replaces. The regenerated `deploy/setup-user-cursor.sh` passes the same tests, so the pre-commit step 13 "Deploy template sync" warning is expected.

**Not changed:**
- One `console.error` remains in the profile-preferences block, which `build-deploy.sh` replaces. It's left for C14.
- An uncaught node exception still prints its stack trace only on the console, but it now produces an ERROR row with the exit code.
- The Cursor installer's own `exit` behavior on Windows is unchanged from before.

- PS1 simulation of `setup-user-cursor.ps1`: 5/5 (base: 1/5).
- `check-script-compliance.sh`: 13 PASS.
- Pre-commit: 0 FAIL. Pre-push: 0 FAIL, and step 7 confirms the dotprofile `deploy/` matches a fresh build.
- Logging audit counts are unchanged, because the scanner doesn't count these defect patterns. The stub tests are the evidence.
- Not tested: real Homebrew, winget and Cursor installer runs on macOS and Windows.

######## PR #43 (2026-10-04T20:54:45Z) Logging conformance PR C8: setup-user-hooks (batch C10)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/43  +457/-34 files=3
FILES: plans/linux-install-hardening.md, scripts/setup-user-hooks.ps1, scripts/setup-user-hooks.sh

Part of epic #21 (logging conformance). The verbatim edits, error-handling audit and test evidence are in `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C8 — verbatim edits (batch C10)".

## Batch C10 (#25, #26a–c, #26f, #27b)

**setup-user-hooks.sh**
- Every early exit now writes a `claude hooks` ERROR row: node missing, manifest missing, manifest lists no hooks, registration list failed, and hook script missing.
- The registration-list node exit code is captured.
- `adopt_managed_file` failures are checked and write an ERROR row. Before, they were ignored, or aborted the script under `set -e`.
  - Because the call now sits inside an `if !`, `set -e` is off within the lib function. A failing step there ends in the lib's logged error plus this ERROR row, not an abort.
- The settings merge prints its diagnostics on stdout as `MSG:` / `WARN:` / `DETAIL:` lines, which bash logs. Before, they reached the console only.
- A non-zero node exit, such as a post-write validation failure, writes an ERROR row. Before, it was a silent `set -e` abort.
- The dry run no longer says "see above".

**setup-user-hooks.ps1**
- ERROR rows are written before both early exits.
- `Adopt-ManagedFile` results are checked. Before, they were piped to `Out-Null`.
- The settings.json write runs in try/catch. A failed write or validation writes an ERROR row instead of "Hooks deployed".

**build-deploy.sh is unchanged on purpose.**
- Its hook-deployment replacement has no manifest checks or adopt code.
- The merge section is extracted, so its fixes reach the deploy script.
- The regenerated `deploy/setup-user-hooks.sh` passes the applicable stub tests, so the pre-commit step 13 warning is expected.
- Not changed: the deploy-only PS1 hook writes in `build-deploy.sh` still have no try/catch. That's noted for C14.

- Stub-driven `setup-user-hooks.sh` tests: 7/7 (base: 1/7). They run on per-case copies of `scripts/` and `shared/`.
- PS1 simulation: 5/5 (base: 1/5).
- The generated deploy script: 4/4.
- `check-script-compliance.sh`: 13 PASS.
- Pre-commit: 0 FAIL. Pre-push: 0 FAIL, and step 7 confirms the dotprofile `deploy/` matches a fresh build.
- Logging audit: A5 21 → 14 (seven early exits now write a row) and A4 282 → 280.
- Not tested: the adopt paths, which need an interactive choice, and real Windows runs.

######## PR #44 (2026-10-04T21:04:44Z) Logging conformance PR C9: setup-user-settings, setup-gh-cli (batch C11)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/44  +440/-43 files=4
FILES: plans/linux-install-hardening.md, scripts/setup-gh-cli.sh, scripts/setup-user-settings.ps1, scripts/setup-user-settings.sh

Part of epic #21 (logging conformance). The verbatim edits, error-handling audit and test evidence are in `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C9 — verbatim edits (batch C11)".

## Batch C11 (#26a–c, #27a–b, #31 caller half)

**setup-user-settings.sh / .ps1**
- The "No settings.json yet -- nothing to sync" branch is removed (#31, C-F5). On a fresh HOME the sync now runs and the lib creates `settings.json` from the profile without prompting. This gets a new row, `OK "claude settings" "created"`.
- When node is missing, an ERROR row is written before `exit 1`.
- A failed sync writes `ERROR "claude settings" "sync failed"`. This covers a corrupt settings.json or profile.json, and apply or validation failures.
  - In bash this used to be a `set -e` abort with no row.
  - In PS1 it used to be `OK "verified"` after `LogError`. PS1 now compares the error count before and after the sync.
- The legacy-key probe no longer suppresses stderr. An unreadable profile.json (exit 2) is logged as detail plus a WARN. Before, it was silently read as "no legacy keys".
- The legacy migration's output and exit code are captured. A failure is logged with a WARN pointing to the log; before, the stack trace went only to the console.

**setup-user-settings.ps1: bug fix**
- The legacy migration never worked on Windows. The script runs as `node <file> <profile>`, so `process.argv[1]` was the script itself, and node failed parsing it as JSON.
  - Every run backed up profile.json, printed a stack trace to the console, and left the legacy key in place.
  - It now reads `argv[2]`. The new exit-code check is what exposed the bug.

**setup-gh-cli.sh (macOS branch)**
- When Homebrew is missing, an ERROR row is written before `exit 1`.
- `brew upgrade gh` now checks the exit code first, keeping the Standard 3 grep. The pattern matches `setup-vercelcli.sh` (C8).
  - Before, a non-zero exit with no "error"/"fatal" text gave OK. The ERROR row now includes the exit code.
  - "see log above" is replaced by the log path.
- A failed `brew install gh` no longer falls through to an OK row.
- The version text comes from `gh_version_line`, so a non-gh banner reads "version unknown".

**build-deploy.sh is unchanged.** Both scripts are extracted or copied as-is, so the regenerated `deploy/` carries the fixes.

## Testing (Linux)
- Bash stub tests: 11/11 (base: 4/11). They cover `setup-user-settings.sh` in 7 cases and the `setup-gh-cli.sh` macOS branch through a `uname` stub in 4 cases.
- PS1 simulation of `setup-user-settings.ps1`: 6/6 (base: 1/6).
- The same bash tests against the generated `deploy/` scripts: 11/11.
- `check-script-compliance.sh`: 13 PASS.
- Pre-commit: 0 FAIL. Pre-push: 0 FAIL, and step 7 confirms the dotprofile `deploy/` matches a fresh build.
- Logging audit: A5 14 → 11, A4 280 → 277, A3 185 → 184, A9 8 → 7.
- Not tested: the Linux apt paths in `setup-gh-cli.sh` (unchanged), the per-leaf prompts (they need a TTY), and real Windows or macOS runs.

Companion dotprofile PR: nobul-jose/aitools-nobul-jose#13. It now also carries the C11 regeneration of `deploy/`.

######## PR #45 (2026-10-04T21:08:57Z) PR C10: installer config merge (PS1), Refresh-Path, build-deploy prefs, reference fixes (batch C11b)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/45  +751/-103 files=13
FILES: plans/linux-install-hardening.md, reference/README.md, reference/framework-hook-rollout.md, reference/framework-incident-governance.md, reference/framework-incident-investigation.md, reference/framework-intent-documentation.md, reference/framework-managed-file-deployment.md, reference/framework-provenance.md, reference/framework-source-of-truth.md, reference/framework-tool-ops.md, reference/tool-ops-claude-code.md, scripts/aitools-install.ps1, scripts/build-deploy.sh

This fixes the findings from a full read of `reference/*`, the entry points, both installers, both libs and `build-deploy.sh`. The verbatim edits, error-handling audit and tests are in `plans/linux-install-hardening.md`, section "PR C10 — verbatim edits (batch C11b, D-C10)".

## Code

**`aitools-install.ps1` Step 5 now uses read-then-merge.** This is the PS1 half of RCA #1, and brings it into parity with `aitools-install.sh` (C-F4, `config-file-safety.md`).
- Before, Step 5 rebuilt `config.json` from scratch. Every key except `userRepoPath`, `machineAlias` and `googleDrives` was dropped, an invalid file was replaced without a trace, and `-DryRun` still wrote.
- Managed keys are `version`, `reposPath`, `repoPath` and `googleDrives`. `googleDrives` is set only when drives were detected; otherwise the existing array is kept, or `[]` is written.
- All other keys are preserved, and key order is kept.
- If nothing changed, nothing is written and the row is OK "verified".
- Invalid JSON is rebuilt, with `userRepoPath` and `machineAlias` salvaged by pattern. The row is WARN "rebuilt (was invalid)", and the invalid copy is backed up.
- The write path is: temp file, then `ValidateJsonConfig`, then `Backup-File`, then move. A read, write or validation failure leaves the existing file untouched and writes an ERROR row naming the stage.
- Changed keys are logged as old → new, with a DETAIL row for each.

**`aitools-install.ps1`: the local `Refresh-Path` override is removed.** It replaced `$env:Path` with the registry value, which dropped session additions for the installer and for every child started after Step 8. The lib's additive version is used now; it is already dot-sourced.

**`build-deploy.sh`: the dead Claude-prefs parsing is removed.**
- It read the legacy `claude.autoMemory`, `alwaysThinking` and `effortLevel` keys, which have moved to `claude.settings.*`.
- It logged wrong values: `autoMemory=true effortLevel=` for a profile with `autoMemoryEnabled: false` and `effortLevel: high`.
- Nothing consumed those values. The generated `deploy/` is byte-identical before and after.

## Docs (D-C10)
- **`reference/README.md`** (not protected): rewritten to list every current file. It still listed two files deleted in b504c43 and covered only 6 of the 39 current files.
- **`reference/tool-ops-claude-code.md`**: removed the cross-reference to the deleted `gh-issue-7490-comment.md`.
- **`reference/framework-*.md`** (8 files) now cite the `/frameworks`, `/incident`, `/tool-ops` and `/glossary` skills instead of registry JSON paths, per `governed-data-access.md`.
  - `@reference/framework-registry.json` was also the wrong path; the registry lives under `registries/`.
  - Intent statements are untouched.

## Testing (Linux)
- **Step 5 tests: 7/7 (base: 2/7).** Step 5 is extracted from the installer and run after the lib under pwsh. The cases:
  - fresh config
  - an unmanaged key is kept and gets a DETAIL row
  - an unchanged file is neither written nor backed up
  - existing drives are kept
  - invalid JSON is rebuilt with key salvage
  - a validation failure leaves the file untouched
  - a dry run writes nothing
- **Build:** `build-deploy.sh` from base and from this change produces a byte-identical `deploy/`, and dotprofile `deploy/` is unchanged.
- **Checks:**
  - `check-script-compliance.sh`: 13 PASS.
  - Pre-commit: 0 FAIL.
  - Pre-push: 0 FAIL, and step 7 passes.
- **Not tested:**
  - a full Windows installer run, since Steps 0–22 need winget
  - `Refresh-Path` on Windows; every setup script already uses the lib version

######## PR #46 (2026-10-05T02:29:40Z) Add Google Chrome as a managed tool for the Claude Code web environment (D-CHR1..3)

Commander-approved batch (2026-10-05), written verbatim from the approved drafts.

| File | Change |
|---|---|

| `reference/tool-registry.md` | +79/-2: matching human-readable entries |
| dotprofile `environments/claude-code-web/CLAUDE.md` | Companion PR: https://github.com/nobul-jose/aitools-nobul-jose/pull/14 |

### Decisions

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D-CHR1 | Google Chrome is a managed tool (chrome-devtools-mcp officially supports Google Chrome and Chrome for Testing only). First supported in the Claude Code web environment (platform: Linux). |
| D-CHR2 | In the Claude Code web environment, chrome-devtools-mcp runs Chrome as root with `--headless` and `--chromeArg=--no-sandbox` (option R4). |
| D-CHR3 | Chrome's runtime libraries, `libnss3-tools`, and root's NSS store with the agent-proxy CA are managed dependencies in that environment. |

## Why

chrome-devtools-mcp could not launch a browser in this environment: there was no Chrome at the default path, sessions run as root, there is no display, and Chrome did not trust the agent-proxy CA. Documentation reads that `web-sources.md` routes through chrome-devtools fell back to WebFetch.

## Tested (Linux, Claude Code web container)

- Drafts copied byte-identical (`cmp`); registry JSON parses (18 tools, 4 MCP servers).
- `scripts/check-pre-commit.sh`: 7 PASS, 11 SKIP, 2 WARN, 0 FAIL.
  - WARN 9 (release notes): caused by this change, because the registry JSON is not `.md`. There is no RELEASE_NOTES entry because the approved batch did not include one; decide at review.
  - WARN 11 (user repo changes): untracked session-archive transcripts in the dotprofile clone, not related to this change.
- R4 applied to this container's `~/.claude.json` and verified with a stdio MCP client (see the session report).

## Not done (follow-ups listed in the reference)

- Onboarding checklist (`tool-lifecycle.md`) for Google Chrome: setup script (`scripts/setup-google-chrome.sh`, Linux; `.ps1` decision for Windows), installer step, `build-deploy.sh` block, check-script `TOOL_CMDS`, CLAUDE.md Managed CLI Tools rows.
- MCP setup (`setup-user-mcp`, `setup-cursor-ide-mcp`): write the D-CHR2 arguments when `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true`, so `aitools install` stops overwriting them.
- Registry schema: platform keys only; environment-specific support goes under a new `environments` key (schema change).
- `/tool-ops` registry entry for Google Chrome, if its governance modes are needed.

######## PR #47 (2026-10-05T03:01:28Z) Sync hook-rollout rule with deployed hooks; delegation-duty decisions (D-DEL1–3)

This PR adds two pieces of governance work as separate commits. Its companion is nobul-jose/aitools-nobul-jose#15, which adds the delegation rule, the CLAUDE.md template section, and the regenerated `deploy/`.

## 1. Hook-rollout rule now matches the deployed hooks (2ed7122)

`.claude/rules/hook-rollout.md` had drifted from the deployed `standing-order-guard.sh`:
- The rule described `||`, `;` and backticks as observe-only under a `MODE_REST` variable. Every check has been enforcing since 2026-03-24 (154fd46), and each check has its own mode variable.
- The rule pointed log review at `~/.claude/hooks/logs/`. The hook has logged to `~/.aitools/logs` since 5a42255.

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `.claude/rules/hook-rollout.md` | Phases and Implementation sections; the enforcement-state table (real variables, the seven checks with no variable, delegation-duty-guard); log path |

| `reference/framework-hook-rollout.md` | Log path |
| `shared/skills/aitool-ops/SKILL.md` | standing-order-guard row |
| `registries/incidents.json` | Incident #61, which records the drift and proposes a check that compares the rule table with the hook's `MODE_*` lines |

## 2. Delegation duty: D-DEL1–D-DEL3 (89e3f75)

The commander's decisions, 2026-10-05:
- **D-DEL1:** On every platform and in every environment, delegation duty includes asking delegates to write each `*.md` file with their name as a prefix (`S2-report.md`).
- **D-DEL2:** There is a delegation rule. It is user-level, in the dotprofile (#15).
- **D-DEL3:** There is a delegation skill.

The reason: Claude Code 2.1.289's Write tool refuses sub-agent writes to files named `REPORT*`, `SUMMARY*`, `FINDINGS*` or `ANALYSIS*` with a `.md` extension. This behaviour is built in and undocumented. The prefix also records which delegate produced each file.

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `shared/skills/delegate/` (new) | User-level `/delegate` skill: the seven duty elements, a prompt skeleton, environment limits, and checking what comes back; plus tests |
| `shared/hooks/delegation-duty-guard.sh` | Seventh element (`md-prefix`) and a `MODE_DUTY` switch, still in observe mode. It now reads the whole input, so pretty-printed JSON no longer scores 0, and it skips calls that aren't Agent calls. |
| `.claude/rules/hook-rollout.md` | delegation-duty-guard row |
| `shared/claude-shared.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `shared/cursor-rules/user-rules.md` | Delegation Duty section or pointer (CLAUDE.md is 84 lines) |
| `reference/tool-ops-claude-code.md`, `registries/tool-ops.json` | Item #26 (sub-agent `.md` name block) and item #27 (`CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH`); hook, KPI and verification cases |
| `.claude/rules/glossary.md`, `registries/glossary.json` | New terms: delegate, delegation duty |
| `handoff`, `aitool-continue`, `aitool-ops`, `mission-control`, `planning` skills; `reference/plan-execution-detail.md` | D-DEL1 added. `handoff` sub-agent outputs are now prefixed. `aitool-continue`'s "No depth limit on recursion" is corrected: depth is capped by `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH`, which defaults to 3 and is 1 in the Claude Code web environment. |

## Testing

######## PR #50 (2026-10-21T02:54:28Z) Derive build-deploy hook preflight from hooks-manifest.json (closes #9)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/50  +155/-58 files=7
FILES: .github/workflows/check.yml, reference/post-push-checklist.md, scripts/build-deploy.sh, scripts/check-hook-parity.py, scripts/check-post-push.ps1, scripts/check-post-push.sh, shared/hooks/hooks-manifest.json

Closes nobul-tech/aitools#9.

## Summary

`build-deploy.sh` checked hook files against a hardcoded preflight list that had drifted from `shared/hooks/hooks-manifest.json` (`block-explore-agent.sh` was missing). It now reads the manifest, and the parity audit checks that the hardcoded list doesn't come back.

| Commit | Change |
|---|---|
| 303a7ec | Read the hook/helper preflight from `hooks-manifest.json` (`hooks[].file` + `deploy[]`, looked up in `shared/hooks/`, then `scripts/`). Remove the dead `HOOK_*` and `AITOOLS_LIB_*` variables. `check-hook-parity.py` takes an optional 4th argument, `build-deploy.sh`, and post-push step 32 (sh + ps1) passes it. |
| e3cab6e | When node is missing, warn and skip the build (exit 0), as origin/main already did, so `aitools` Step 2 doesn't stop before the installer installs node. A missing or unparseable manifest still exits 1. Both embed loops now exit 1 on a missing source instead of `continue`. |
| 4dd4af8 | Parity check: ignore full-line comments and match whole names only (`(?<![\w.-])NAME(?![\w.-])`). "Reads hooks-manifest.json" must appear outside comments. Add type hints and catch `TypeError`. |
| 3d8c455 | Fix check-post-push step 27: `paste -sd ', '` was missing its `-` operand, so BSD paste aborted the checklist before step 32. Correct the step 32 header comments (sh + ps1). |
| 946f733 | Document step 32 in `reference/post-push-checklist.md`. Fix `hooks-manifest.json` `meta.reference`, which pointed at a plan file that doesn't exist. |
| d993a0f | CI: run `check-hook-parity.py` with all four arguments in the Linux job. |

## Verification (macOS)

- Builds ran with an isolated HOME pointed at a copy of the dotprofile, never the real one. They exit 0 with `Preflight OK: 20 hook/helper file(s)`. Output is identical under bash 5 and `/bin/bash` 3.2.
- Compared with an origin/main build, `diff -r` shows a change only in the `# Hooks deployed:` header comment of `deploy/setup-user-hooks.{sh,ps1}`.
- pwsh ParseFile passes for `check-post-push.ps1` and for all 21 generated `deploy/*.ps1`.
- Parity, 4 args: `bash + ps1 + build-deploy generate hook lists from manifest (18 hooks)`. Against origin/main's `build-deploy.sh` it flags 19 hardcoded names: every hook except `block-explore-agent.sh`, which is the drift.
- Negative tests, 10/10 as expected:
  - renamed hook → exit 1
  - literal hook name in code → fail; in a comment → pass; `my-session-archive.sh` and other lookalikes → pass
  - manifest only named in a comment → fail
  - malformed manifest → clean error
  - no node → exit 0 with a warning
  - unparseable or missing manifest → exit 1
  - embed loop reached with a missing source → exit 1
- `check-post-push.sh` (extensive) now gets past step 27 and **step 32 passes**. Its remaining FAILs come from the isolated HOME (no MCP configs) and the branch not yet being on main.

Not run: the PS1 step 32 on Windows (parse-checked only), and CI (this PR is its first run).

- The dotprofile `deploy/` needs a companion regeneration commit (comment-only change in `setup-user-hooks.{sh,ps1}`) for pre-push step 7.
- Release notes entry goes in the v0.74.0 release.
- Pre-existing: step 27's `paste -sd ', '` alternates delimiters (`a,b c`) when 3 or more prereqs are missing. If build-deploy fails after its `rm -rf`, the dotprofile `deploy/` stays half-built until the next good build.

######## PR #55 (2026-10-22T00:43:03Z) Record macOS verification of check-pre-push step 7 (closes #32, fixed in 7aacdef)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/55  +253/-0 files=1
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Path32/S3-Path32-verification.md

## Summary

Closes #32.

Issue #32 was fixed on main in 7aacdef (2026-10-03, "Pre-push step 7: verify the dotprofile deploy/ against a fresh build"). That commit never referenced the issue, so the issue stayed open. Step 7 in `scripts/check-pre-push.sh` and `.ps1` now works like this:

- It resolves `userRepoPath` from `$HOME/.aitools/config.json`, the same source `build-deploy.sh` uses.
- It SKIPs when `userRepoPath` is unset or not a git repo.
- Otherwise it rebuilds into a temp copy of the dotprofile's committed tree under a temp HOME and compares `deploy/`. PASS when the copy matches. FAIL names the stale files.
- It never requires the frozen `aitools/deploy/`.

7aacdef was tested on Linux only. This PR adds a macOS verification record for both scripts. **No script changes**: the code already does what the mission brief asked for.

The PR title differs from the brief ("Fix check-pre-push step 7 ..."), because there was nothing left to fix.

X-B2 (M3) will revisit this step. It must pass `AITOOLS_USER_REPO_PATH` explicitly, unset `AITOOLS_PROFILE_FILE` / `AITOOLS_MACHINE_ALIAS`, and add `aitools-profile.js` to the trigger regex.

## Commits

- e1cd82d Record macOS verification of check-pre-push step 7 (closes #32)

## Verification

Full record: `.aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Path32/S3-Path32-verification.md`. It includes the harness verbatim.

The whole of `check-pre-push.sh` ran on macOS 27.0.1, and so did a copy of `check-pre-push.ps1` with its OS guard stripped. Each ran under an isolated HOME, with a fake dotprofile under the worktree scratch dir:

| Case | bash | PS1 |
|------|------|-----|
| no config.json | SKIP, exit 0 | SKIP, exit 0 |
| userRepoPath not a git repo | SKIP, exit 0 | SKIP, exit 0 |
| dotprofile deploy/ stale (setup-pandoc changed) | FAIL `(setup-pandoc.ps1 setup-pandoc.sh)`, exit 1 | identical |
| deploy/ rebuilt from branch | PASS, exit 0 | PASS, exit 0 |
| stray file in committed deploy/ | FAIL `(stray-file.txt)`, exit 1 | identical |

The real dotprofile `deploy/` content hash was unchanged before and after the run (`31212e5d56de`). `bash -n scripts/check-pre-push.sh` passed.

## Not run

- `check-pre-commit.sh`: the auto-mode classifier denied running it. The isolated-HOME form was refused by worktree isolation.
- `check-pre-push.sh` on this branch: not run, for the same reason. This push has no deploy-relevant files, so step 7 would SKIP.
- Standalone pwsh parser check: blocked. The PS1 still parsed and ran to completion under pwsh in all 5 cases.
- The rebuild-failure FAIL path, the bash node-missing WARN and the PS1 Git-Bash-missing WARN were not exercised. Windows was not exercised either.

- pre-commit step 3 `--fix` still runs `build-deploy.sh` under the real HOME, then runs `git add deploy/` in aitools.

FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-claude/rules/hook-lifecycle.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-claude/rules/hook-rollout.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-claude/rules/hook-rollout.md.diff, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-cursor/rules/hook-lifecycle.mdc, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-cursor/rules/hook-rollout.mdc, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-cursor/rules/hook-rollout.mdc.diff, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-placement.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/S3-Hooks-verification.md, scripts/setup-user-hooks.ps1, scripts/setup-user-hooks.sh, shared/hooks/standing-order-guard.sh

## Summary

Mission HOOKS (S3-Hooks, for Session Commander 10cf255a-c). Fixes incidents #80, #81, #82.

- **#80 (decision SD-2):** `setup-user-hooks.sh/.ps1` now resolve manifest hooks from `shared/hooks/` then `scripts/` only. That is the same order as the `build-deploy.sh` embed loops, so the dev and MDM paths deploy the same file. A dotprofile copy of a manifest hook is ignored, with a `log_warn` and a `WARN` row ("dotprofile copy ignored"). `[a]dopt` writes `shared/hooks/<file>` only, never the dotprofile, and the adopt label now reads "aitools shared/hooks". This matches the existing spec in `reference/managed-file-deployment.md` "Shell Script Deployment Detail".
- **#81:** the reverse-discovery prompt now uses `tty_interactive` + `read_tty_choice` (bash) and `Test-InteractiveConsole` + `Read-ConsoleChoice` (PS1). EOF at the prompt logs a warning and defaults to `[s]kip`, the choice that writes nothing. A non-interactive run logs that it skipped the prompt.
- **#82:** `standing-order-guard.sh` lets read-only `sed -n '<N[,M]>p' <file>` through. The match is narrow: numeric or `$` addresses only, and no `-i`, `w` or redirection. Per `hook-rollout.md`, a matching-logic change resets the check, so the sed check gets a mode variable, `MODE_SED`, set to `observe`.
- **`build-deploy.sh`: no change needed.** Its hook section replaces the whole `hook deployment` block. The MDM scripts have no dotprofile lookup, no adopt and no prompt, so there is nothing to mirror. The guard change reaches MDM through the `cat` embed. Verified on an isolated-HOME build.

User-created (non-manifest) hooks still adopt to the dotprofile. This follows #80's suggested resolution ("the dotprofile holds only user-created hooks"). It is an open question for the commander in `S3-Hooks-placement.md`.

## Protected drafts: awaiting approval, NOT applied

All are under `.aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-Hooks/`:

| Draft | Target | Change |
|-------|--------|--------|
| `S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-claude/rules/hook-rollout.md` (+ `.diff`) | `.claude/rules/hook-rollout.md` | `MODE_SED` observe row (#82), an exemption-test step, a log note, a pointer to hook-lifecycle |
| `S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-cursor/rules/hook-rollout.mdc` (+ `.diff`) | `.cursor/rules/hook-rollout.mdc` | Mirror of the above |
| `S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-claude/rules/hook-lifecycle.md` | new `.claude/rules/hook-lifecycle.md` | New rule with an **intent block** (protected). Covers one source per hook, deploy equivalence, the adopt target, user-created hooks, prompts, retirement and verification |
| `S3-Hooks-drafts/dot-cursor/rules/hook-lifecycle.mdc` | new `.cursor/rules/hook-lifecycle.mdc` | Mirror |
| `S3-Hooks-placement.md` | (recommendation) | Recommends a new rule over extending `hook-rollout.md` or `managed-file-deployment.md`, with reasons and the follow-on edits |

Until the hook-rollout draft is approved, `.claude/rules/hook-rollout.md` still says the sed check is enforcing with no mode variable. The code now observes.

## Summary

Restores the standing-order-guard sed check to **enforce** (it regressed to observe in #56) and ships, as **drafts awaiting the commander's approval**, the hook-rollout rule update and the hook-lifecycle rule fixes from #56's review. Once this merges, `aitools` may be run again on this machine: the deployed guard will block `sed -i`, `s///w` and redirect writes outside a pipeline again.

## Why

- #56 added the read-only `sed -n 'N[,M]p' <file>` exemption (incident #82) and reset `MODE_SED` to observe, which re-allowed `sed -i s/a/b/ file.txt` (exit 0 on the new guard, 2 on the old).
- Commander decision Q2 (2026-10-21): keep the sed check at enforce; a change that only narrows what is blocked does not require an observe reset.
- #56 review findings (a)-(e) on the S3-Hooks hook-lifecycle draft and harness.

## Changes

- `shared/hooks/standing-order-guard.sh`: `MODE_SED="enforce"`; header comment (rollout list) says the 2026-10-21 change only narrowed matching, so the check stays at enforce. Exemption regex unchanged.

  - `S3-HooksFix-drafts/dot-claude/rules/hook-rollout.md` + `.diff` vs live: sed row at enforce; `MODE_SED` in the variable list; "a matching change that only narrows what is blocked keeps the current mode; a widening change resets to observe"; reset bullet 2 reworded to "blocks commands it did not block before"; exemption-test step 4; log sentence back to "absent while every check enforces"; cross-ref to the user-level hook-lifecycle rule.
  - `S3-HooksFix-drafts/dot-cursor/rules/hook-rollout.mdc` + `.diff`: same content, condensed.
  - `S3-HooksFix-drafts/shared/rules/hook-lifecycle.md` + diff vs the S3-Hooks draft: **user-level** (per the session commander's location change; no `.mdc` mirror). Fixes: (a) the real-HOME ban is scoped to agent/delegate verification runs and says it does not change deploy-paths.md step 2 / tool-lifecycle.md "Rebuild + propagate"; (b) SD-2 quoted verbatim from the 360aa8f3-8 handoff, plus "the scripts ignore a later dotprofile copy and warn, they do not remove it" (setup-user-hooks.sh:134-135, .ps1:95-96); (c) Prompts: non-interactive reverse discovery logs at **info** (setup-user-hooks.sh:260, .ps1:205), EOF logs a warning via `read_tty_choice` (aitools-lib.sh:791), and `--force` does not affect reverse discovery (`tty_interactive`, aitools-lib.sh:774-779, does not read `AITOOLS_FORCE`). **Intent change (protected):** "Audience" rewritten to user level ("Every agent, in any repo on any machine, that adds, changes, adopts or retires an aitools-managed hook ..."); "(this repo)" removed from the heading; cross-refs to project rules prefixed "aitools". ## Follow-ups

- Recorded limitation (in the smoke test): the guard checks only the first token, so a multi-line command whose first line matches the exemption (`sed -n 1p f` then `sed s/a/b/wout f` on the next line) passes. The same is already true for any first line (`ls` then `sed -i ...`), so the exemption does not open a new class; tightening the exemption to single-line commands would be a narrowing-safe fix.
- Bare tty reads: see `S3-HooksFix-bare-reads.md` (INCIDENT).

Rewrites the /scratch skill to match what the hooks and `scripts/ait-harvest.py` actually do, and adds a **draft** user rule `shared/rules/scratch.md` for the commander's approval. The rule is **not live**: it sits in the mission folder (`S3-ScratchRule-drafts/shared/rules/scratch.md`), because it carries an intent statement (protected) and because nothing deploys `shared/rules/` yet.

Mission SCRATCH-RULE (S3-ScratchRule), Session Commander 10cf255a-c.

## Why

- The skill said the SessionEnd hook deletes scratch and cited `harvest-session.sh` lines 164-166. The shim is 65 lines and the harvester never deletes anything (`ait-harvest.py:752-754`, `:796-798`). `.claude/rules/artifact-harvesting.md` ("no longer deletes -- v0.64.1") was right.
- Its examples (`$(cat ...)`, multi-line heredocs) are blocked by standing-order-guard in enforce mode.
- Its commit example credited an old model.
- It had no delegate or git-worktree case, and told agents to read `.scratch/.current-session`. That pointer is shared by every session in a checkout, and any harvest deletes it.
- Probes found a high-severity harvester bug (S3-SR-8): a session-dir suffix longer than 10 characters makes catch-up harvest the live session's dir instead of the orphan.

## Changes

- `shared/skills/scratch/SKILL.md`: location (`.scratch/session-<10-char id>/`), how to find your dir (the context line first, the pointer only as a checked hint, delegates `mkdir -p` their own), git worktrees, a naming table with each name's harvest outcome, the commit-message and PR-body (`--body-file`) patterns using the Write tool, script pattern for bash and pwsh, the real lifecycle, and a cross-reference to the draft rule. The intent block is unchanged; the description frontmatter is updated.
- `.aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-ScratchRule/`
  - `S3-ScratchRule-findings.md`: each inconsistency with file:line evidence and probe output
  - `S3-ScratchRule-drafts/shared/rules/scratch.md`: draft rule (74 lines)
  - `S3-ScratchRule-deploy-gap.md`: deployment options and recommendation
  - `S3-ScratchRule-incidents.md`: `INCIDENT:` markers S3-SR-1..8 (not filed)
  - `S3-ScratchRule-report.md`: mission report

## Verification

- Probe 1 (throwaway repo, isolated `HOME`, real `ait-harvest.py harvest`): `git worktree remove` exits 0 and deletes an ignored `.scratch/`. The harvester copies `msg-*.txt`, `pr-body.md`, `events.jsonl` and dotfiles, skips `commit-msg.txt` and `*.log`, ignores subdirs, deletes nothing, and removes `.current-session`.
- Probe 2 (`ait-harvest.py catchup`, live dir `session-LIVE012345` named in the pointer, orphan `session-S3-ScratchRule`): the harvested file was the live dir's `live-work.sh`, filed as `session-S3-Scratch_live-work.sh`. The live dir was marked `.harvested`, the pointer was removed, and the orphan was untouched.

scripts/setup-user-claude.sh, scripts/setup-user-hooks.ps1, scripts/setup-user-hooks.sh, scripts/setup-user-skills.ps1, scripts/setup-user-skills.sh, tests/managed-files/pty-run.py, tests/managed-files/test-managed-files.ps1, tests/managed-files/test-managed-files.sh

## Summary

Implements the managed-file model the commander approved on 2026-10-21, in both
shared libraries and every caller:
- SOURCE is the source of truth. There is no ancestor and no deploy state.
- There is one review menu: `[o]verwrite / [a]ccept & adopt / [m]erge & adopt / [s]kip / [x]abort`.
- On adopt or merge, the library writes the repo file in the same step.
- JSON files are reviewed field by field.
- Every write is backed up and snapshotted first and validated after. On a failure the files are restored, the result is `failed` (ERROR row), and the run continues.

This fixes incident #129: an accepted merge was reverted by the next run's silent auto-deploy, and auto-deploy no longer exists. It also removes the cause of #130 (deploy state needed node). Closes #30.

Plan, verbatim blocks, audit and report: `.aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-SourceTruth/`.

## Why

- **Managed-file model** (approved 2026-10-21): mission packet section 6; branch docs/managed-file-choices, `.claude/rules/agentic-standards.md` "Managed-file review choices and merge"; spec drafts in PR #63.
- **Incidents** #129 and #130; **GitHub issues** #29 and #30 (findings below).
- **D-TS13 reversed** by the commander on 2026-10-21: there is no abort marker. `[x]abort` stays `log_error` + `exit 2`, exactly as on main.

## Interface contract (as implemented)

### bash

- **`deploy_managed_file SRC_CONTENT DEST TOOL_NAME ITEM_NAME [ADOPT_PATH] [ADOPT_FILTER]`**
  - Sets `MANAGED_FILE_RESULT` to `created | updated | verified | accept & adopt | skipped | failed`.
  - `ADOPT_PATH` is the repo file that is the source of truth. `""` means adopt and merge are not offered.
  - A DEST ending in `.json` goes to `review_json_fields`.
- **`prompt_diff_review DEST NEW_CONTENT ADOPT_OFFERED(0|1) [CUR_CONTENT]`**
  - Sets `DIFF_REVIEW_RESULT` to `overwrite | adopt | merge | skip | failed`, plus `MERGED_CONTENT` on merge.
  - Non-interactive runs, EOF, and `--force` / `AITOOLS_FORCE` all mean overwrite.
- **`review_json_fields SRC_FILE DEST ADOPT_PATH`**: node compares by key path, and a whole array counts as one field. Key order is kept. A LOCAL-only field stays in LOCAL.
- **Tracker**: `deploy_tracker_record failed` adds to the count and writes `DETAIL failed: <item>`. `deploy_tracker_summary` writes ERROR when failed > 0.
- **`prompt_json_field_review`**: gains optional `$6` (local label, default `settings.json`) and `$7` (overwrite allowed, default 1). `sync_managed_json` behaviour and output are unchanged.

### PS1 twins

- `Deploy-ManagedFile -Content -DestPath -ToolName -ItemName [-AdoptPath] [-AdoptFilter <scriptblock>]`
- `Prompt-DiffReview -FilePath -NewContent -AdoptOffered [-CurrentContent]`
- `Review-JsonFields -SourceFile -DestPath -AdoptPath`
- `Record-DeployOutcome failed`; `Write-DeployTrackerSummary` writes ERROR when failed > 0
- `Prompt-JsonFieldReview -LocalLabel -OverwriteAllowed`

### Removed

- **bash**: `initialize_deploy_state`, `get_deploy_state_hash`, `update_deploy_state`, `get_deploy_shadow`, `_deploy_state_key`, `try_auto_merge`, `AUTO_MERGED_CONTENT`, `_DEPLOY_STATE_DIR`, `_DEPLOY_MANIFEST`, `get_content_hash` (nothing else in the repo used it).
- **PS1**: `Initialize-DeployState`, `Get-DeployState`, `Get-DeployStateKey`, `Update-DeployState`, `Get-DeployShadow`, `Try-AutoMerge`, `Get-ContentHash`, `$script:DeployManifest`, `$script:DeployStateDir`.
- `adopt_managed_file` / `Adopt-ManagedFile` stay; reverse discovery of user-created hooks and skills uses them.
- An existing `~/.aitools/deploy-state/` is left on disk. One detail-level log line per run says it is no longer used.

## Contract change (pending commander decision)

The session commander relayed these defaults from SPEC-REWRITE (PR #63). Each one is a single function or variable to change.

1. **CLAUDE.md adopt transform**: new optional `ADOPT_FILTER` / `-AdoptFilter`. setup-user-claude passes its existing transform (footer stripped, tokens restored).
2. **Failed AI merge**: `prompt_diff_review` reports it as `failed`; `deploy_managed_file` turns that into `failed` with nothing written.
3. **Merge preview `[n]o`** returns to the review menu instead of overwriting.
4. **Mixed per-field JSON choices** give one result per file, by precedence: failed > accept & adopt > skipped > updated > verified.
5. **Restore of a file that did not exist before**: the partial file is removed.
6. **JSON field that exists only in LOCAL**: the review offers `[a]dopt / [s]kip / [x]abort` and no overwrite. Without an adopt target, a terminal, or under `--force`, the field is kept with no prompt.
7. **JSON required keys** are SOURCE's top-level keys, minus any key the review chose to leave absent. Otherwise a deliberate skip of a SOURCE-only key would fail validation.
8. **A failed backup of an existing file** fails the step, and nothing is written. This conflicts with `config-file-safety.md` ("Backup failures are non-fatal"); see Protected-file drafts.
9. **Skills adopt target** is the file actually read (dotprofile copy or `shared/skills`). The MDM embedded deploy has no adopt target.
10. **User-created hooks** are still adopted into the dotprofile through `adopt_managed_file`.
11. **Dotprofile auto-commit and push** after a rule merge & adopt is kept, ported unchanged; the commander confirmed this.
12. **cli-config.json** (setup-user-cursor's own merge) is untouched.
13. **CLAUDE.md with no dotprofile template**: when SOURCE came from `shared/claude-shared.md`, adopt creates the dotprofile template, as on main.

My additions:
- `prompt_json_field_review` `$6`/`$7` and `Prompt-JsonFieldReview -LocalLabel/-OverwriteAllowed`.
- After every adopt, the library logs "Uncommitted change in <repo>" (it never commits).
- An adopt target listed in its repo's protected-files table gets a WARN that names it.

## Changes

- **`scripts/aitools-lib.sh` / `.ps1`**: the deploy-state, auto-merge, review, deploy, tracker and field-prompt code is replaced (plan section 2). New helpers:
  - `_managed_abort` / `Invoke-ManagedAbort` (log_error + exit 2)
  - `_managed_write` / `Write-ManagedFiles` (snapshot, backup, write, validate, restore)
  - `_managed_adopt_notice` / `_managed_protected_note` and their PS1 twins
  - `review_json_fields` / `Review-JsonFields`, which share one embedded node script, byte-identical in both libraries
- **Bug fix, `_merge_validate` (bash)**: it always returned 0 because its last command was an assignment, so bash AI-merge validation never rejected any output. It now returns the validator's status. The PS1 wrapper was already correct.
- **PS1 now compares case-sensitively** (`-ceq`, `Compare-Object -CaseSensitive`). Before, a case-only local edit counted as "verified".
- **`scripts/setup-user-claude.sh` / `.ps1`**:
  - CLAUDE.md now passes the dotprofile adopt path and an adopt filter. Empty profile values are never substituted; before, an empty company matched everywhere.
  - Rules pass their adopt path; the manual adopt copy and the merged write-back are gone; `failed` is handled.
  - Validation skips files whose deploy failed.
- **`scripts/setup-user-skills.sh` / `.ps1`**:
  - The adopt target is the file read.
  - The copy to the other deploy target is backed up and checked, with an ERROR row on failure.
  - Reverse discovery uses `tty_interactive` + `read_tty_choice` (and the PS1 twins).
- **`scripts/setup-user-hooks.sh` / `.ps1`**:
  - The adopt target is the hook's own source file. Before, adopting `ait-harvest.py` (which lives in `scripts/`) created a shadowing copy in `shared/hooks`.
  - chmod is checked.
- **`scripts/build-deploy.sh`**: the embedded-skill deploy passes no adopt target; the manual adopt and sync code is removed.
- **`scripts/check-post-push.sh` / `.ps1`**: step 28 (deploy state integrity) is retired and reports SKIP.
- **`tests/managed-files/`** (new): `test-managed-files.sh`, `test-managed-files.ps1`, and `pty-run.py`, which drives the prompts on a pseudo-terminal and uses a stub `claude` for the AI merge.
- **`.github/workflows/check.yml`**: runs the new suites on macOS, Linux and Windows. On Windows the pty and read-only cases are skipped.

## Verification

All on macOS (bash 5 and /bin/bash 3.2, pwsh 7):

- **Syntax**: `bash -n` and `/bin/bash -n` pass on every touched `.sh`, the pwsh parser passes on every touched `.ps1`, and `py_compile` passes on `pty-run.py`.
- **Exact apply**: all 11 code files are byte-identical to a dry apply of the plan's verbatim blocks on origin/main copies. The PS1 files compare equal after CRLF->LF and have 0 bare-LF lines.
- **`bash tests/managed-files/test-managed-files.sh`**: 42 passed, 0 failed. The cases:
  - created, verified, non-interactive overwrite, deploy-state notice
  - read-only directory gives `failed` and the run continues; a failed backup gives `failed`; tracker ERROR row
  - `--force` and EOF; no adopt target gives an `[o/s/x]` menu; skip
  - adopt, then the next run verifies; adopt filter
  - merge & adopt (stub AI), then the next run verifies (#129)
  - merge `[n]o` returns to the menu; a failed AI merge gives `failed` with nothing written
  - an adopt-target write failure restores LOCAL; abort exits 2
  - JSON: created, non-interactive, LOCAL-only kept, invalid LOCAL; per-field o/a/s plus LOCAL-only adopt; precedence; JSON restore
  - CLAUDE.md through setup-user-claude.sh under an isolated HOME: created, then adopt into the template with the token restored and no footer, then verified
- **`pwsh -NoProfile -File tests/managed-files/test-managed-files.ps1`**: 23 passed, 0 failed. Same scope, at library level.
- **`tests/logging`**: bash 24/24; PS1 15 passed, 5 skipped (the skips are Windows-only).
- **`build-deploy.sh`** under an isolated HOME with a fake dotprofile: exit 0, and all 42 generated scripts parse. The embedded skills pass an empty adopt path.
- **`check-post-push.sh`** under an isolated HOME:
  - 28 SKIP (retired)
  - 29 PASS: menus match a,m,n,o,s,x
  - 30 PASS: callers handle created, failed, skipped, updated, verified
  - 31 PASS: PS1 and bash return values match
  - The other FAILs and WARNs come from the empty fake HOME (no MCP configs, tool versions).
- **`check-pre-commit.sh`** (no `--fix`, incident #136):
  - FAIL step 6: pre-existing; `harvesting/2026-07-09_session-ba4c0c12-7_build-deploy-prefix.sh` is tracked 100644.
  - WARN 3 (build freshness), 5, 7, 9 and 11 (uncommitted changes in the real dotprofile; see Follow-ups).
- **`check-pre-push.sh`**: FAIL step 7 (dotprofile `deploy/` stale). It stays stale until `build-deploy.sh` is run and committed in the dotprofile after this PR merges. Everything else is PASS or WARN.
- **Independent review**: S3-SourceTruth-Audit reviewed the plan before execution; its findings file is in the mission folder. Fixed before execution:
  - H1: the apply tools now keep CRLF files CRLF
  - L3: PS1 CLAUDE.md filter parity
  - L6: the restore-state marker write is checked
- **Batch reviews**: two batch delegates found a missing ERROR row on a failed skills sync copy (fixed) and a stale comment (fixed).

## Issue findings

- **#30 (closed by this PR)**: every row in it is now addressed.
  - The diff goes through `log_detail` at every size (already on main, `prompt_diff_review`).
  - `CheckLogInit` derives the OS (already on main, `check-lib.ps1:52`).
  - Backups are guarded (already on main, `backup_file`).
  - The unchecked node calls in deploy state are gone, because deploy state is removed here.
- **#29 (not closed)**:
  - The library prompts all read through `read_tty_choice` / `Read-ConsoleChoice`, and the PS1 side uses one detector, `Test-InteractiveConsole`. This was already on main.
  - This PR fixes the last bare read on that path, in `setup-user-skills` reverse discovery (`read -r choice < /dev/tty` aborted under `set -e` at EOF), plus its PS1 twin.
  - It adds an EOF regression test on a pty for the text review prompt in both languages.
  - Why not closed: the JSON-field and merge prompts use the same helper but have no EOF test of their own, and entry points outside the library still use bare reads (`scripts/aitools:108,157,957,1058-1066,1118-1128,1181`, `scripts/relay-outbound-prompt.sh:77`).

## Not run (and why)

- **Windows**: Git Bash, Windows pwsh and the Windows-guarded `.ps1` setup scripts were parse-checked only; no Windows machine is available here. CI will run the new suites on Windows.
- **A real AI merge**: the tests use a stub `claude`.
- **`aitools` / `aitools install`** on this machine: forbidden until HOOKS-FIX merges, and never against the real HOME.
- **`check-pre-commit --fix`**: it runs build-deploy with the real HOME (#136).

## Protected-file drafts awaiting approval

- **`.claude/rules/config-file-safety.md`**, "Backup before overwrite" (only if contract item 8 is kept). Old: "Backup failures are non-fatal (warn and proceed) -- a failed backup should not block deployment." Proposed: "Backup failures are non-fatal for whole-directory backups (warn and proceed). For a managed file written by `deploy_managed_file` / `Deploy-ManagedFile`, a failed backup of an existing file fails that file (nothing written, ERROR row) and the run continues."
- **`reference/managed-file-deployment.md`, `.claude/rules/managed-file-deployment.md`, `interactive-menus.md`**: the text is owned by mission SPEC-REWRITE (PR #63). Its drafts still mention `AITOOLS_ABORT_FILE` / D-TS13 (audit L5) and need the reversal applied. On main, these files still describe Try-AutoMerge and deploy state until #63 lands.

## Follow-ups

- **D-TS13**: reversed, so there is no runner follow-up.
- **Repo `.bak.*` files** (audit L1): adopt targets now get rotated `.bak.<ts>` backups inside repo working trees (dotprofile rules and CLAUDE.md, shared/skills, shared/hooks, scripts/). These are not gitignored. Options: add `*.bak.20*` to both repos' .gitignore, or skip the rotated backup for adopt targets tracked by git (keep only the restore snapshot).
- **Corrupt LOCAL JSON** (audit M2): it fails on every run and is never repaired. This is latent today, because no caller deploys JSON through `deploy_managed_file`. Option: treat it as a root-level difference, so a non-interactive run overwrites it with a backup.
- **Bash failed-merge reason** (audit M4, INCIDENT): it always reads "unknown error". `invoke_ai` runs inside `$(...)`, so `AI_REJECT_REASON` is lost; this is the same on main.
- **PS1 console input after the AI spinner**: under the pty test harness, the first menu read after `Start-AiSpinner`'s job comes back corrupted. The test asserts only that the menu returns and nothing is written. Unknown whether a real terminal shows this.
- **Check step 30**: it tokenizes return values with `\w[\w-]*`, so it never checks "accept & adopt". This is pre-existing.
- **Dead code**: `ALL_SKILL_DESTS` / `$allSkillDests` in build-deploy are no longer read.
- **Dotprofile**: rebuild and commit the dotprofile `deploy/` after merge (pre-push step 7).

## Summary

Ships the approved `/tool-adopt` skill at user level (`shared/skills/tool-adopt/SKILL.md`, deployed to `~/.claude/skills/`) and the approved tool-support gate glossary terms in `registries/glossary.json`. Mission TOOL-ADOPT (S3-ToolAdopt), Session Commander 10cf255a-c.

## Why

Decisions in `plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md`:

- **TA-PLACE**: user level. Commander note: "it should not be process only, it should do work to evaluate (if needed) and adopt the tool". The skill now says so in a new section, "This skill does the work".
- **TA-SKILL**: the S3-GateFix draft, approved as written.
- **TA-INTENT**: the intent, verbatim.
- **TA-M2**: the "Today and after M2" table kept as drafted.
- **TA-GLOSSARY, G-provenance (b), N-Q3, G-userconfig, G-profile, G-verified, G-recovered, C-14 (a)**: the glossary entries below.

## Changes

### `shared/skills/tool-adopt/SKILL.md` (new)

Copied from `.aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-GateFix/S3-GateFix-drafts/shared/skills/tool-adopt/SKILL.md` with these changes:

1. Removed the `> DRAFT ...` banner. It said "process-only skill", which TA-PLACE overrides.
2. Added a section after the intent:

   > ## This skill does the work
   >
   > `/tool-adopt` is not a checklist to read: invoking it means you carry the tool to supported in this run. You do each of these yourself:
   >
   > - **Evaluate when needed**: when a target has no approved preferred method, run the `/tool-eval` skill now, with the commander, and bring its result back here (step 2).
   > - **Write the registry entry**: draft every missing field and write it through the `/tool-registry` skill once the commander approves (step 3).
   > - **Write the setup scripts**: create or migrate `scripts/setup-<tool>.sh` and `.ps1` (step 4), wire them into the installers and build (step 5), and test them (step 6).
   >
   > Stop only at an approval gate (the `/tool-eval` Phase 2 gate, a protected file) or when a step waits for M2; report what remains.
3. Renamed the table column "Today (process only)" to "Today (current rules, hand-written scripts)".

The intent block is unchanged and matches TA-INTENT. The skill names no registry JSON path (governed-data-access.md). It reaches the registry only through `/tool-registry`.

### `registries/glossary.json`

Seven terms added, appended in the file's insertion order. One term extended. `meta.lastUpdated` set to 2026-10-21.

#### tool adoption (new; TA-GLOSSARY, verbatim)
- definition: The /tool-adopt process that makes a managed tool supported on a platform and environment: an approved preferred method in the tool registry (running /tool-eval when there is none), the registry data its setup scripts need (after M2: the data the tool support decision tree reads), setup scripts in the aitools repo, installer and build integration, and verification. Runs after /tool-eval. Not the managed-file review choices '[a]ccept & adopt' and '[m]erge & adopt' (the deployed file, or an AI merge, copied back to the repo, the source of truth), and not framework 'adoption' (mapping a discipline's concepts into the harness).
- source: shared/skills/tool-adopt/SKILL.md; reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md (after M2)

#### install provenance (new; G-provenance option b)
- definition: Where the installed copy of a managed tool came from: the install method and upstream distribution (vendor installer, package manager formula or id, crate, or unknown) and who publishes it. Identified on a machine by the method's detection signature (its result is the install class in the tool support decision tree) and verified before adoption by the /tool-eval provenance checks (official endorsement, publisher, signing). Also called tool distribution provenance. Not 'provenance', the aitools component that tracks what every harness artifact is based on.
- source: /tool-eval skill; reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md sections 1 and 5 (after M2); decision G-provenance (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)

#### detection signature (new; C-14 option a)
- definition: The typed, read-only probes on one install method in the tool registry (JSON key 'detection': one probe, or an array whose probes must all pass, such as brew-formula:<name>, winget:<id>, path:<glob>) that identify which method installed the binary a command resolves to. The library implements each probe kind; the registry never holds shell code. Not 'detection', the hook layer of three-layer governance.
- source: /tool-registry skill; reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md section 5 (after M2); decision C-14 (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)

#### machine id (new; N-Q3 rename of 'machine identity')
- definition: The platform's stable machine identifier (macOS IOPlatformUUID, Windows MachineGuid, Linux machine-id), stored raw (not hashed) as the machine id in a machine profile inside profile.json and matched by the machine resolver (scripts/aitools-profile.sh/.ps1) to choose this machine's machine profile. Hosted environments such as Claude Code web have none and match by environment. Not the user's identity (profile.json identity).
- source: reference/user-repo.md (after M3); decisions N-Q3, P-Q2, P-Q3 (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)
- Note: the S4-Review draft defined it as an HMAC hash computed by aitools-profile.js. That text is replaced to follow P-Q2 (raw ids) and P-Q3 (native resolver).

#### aitools config (new; N-Q3 rename of 'derived config', meaning per G-userconfig)
- definition: The aitools harness config, for short: all managed configuration data aitools deploys and keeps for a user, for every tool it manages. It includes Claude Code configuration (settings, CLAUDE.md, rules, skills, hooks), Cursor and Cursor CLI configuration, shell variables and aliases, and ~/.aitools/config.json itself (per-machine bootstrap pointers plus values derived from the profile). A standalone term, not a composed '<scope modifier> config'.
- source: decisions N-Q3 and G-userconfig (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md); reference/user-repo.md

#### profile (new; G-profile)
- definition: The user profile: profile.json in the user's dotprofile repo, holding the user's preferences, identity, and machine profiles. A machine profile is a field inside it (one entry of its 'profiles' object, keyed by machine alias), not a separate file. Not a shell profile (.zshrc, .bashrc, PowerShell $PROFILE), which is always called a shell profile.
- source: decision G-profile (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md); reference/user-repo.md

#### recovered (new; G-recovered)
- definition: Deployment outcome: the existing file was not valid JSON, so the writer rebuilt it from the keys it could salvage plus freshly derived values, after backing up the invalid copy. Reported as WARN ('rebuilt (was invalid)'). Used for ~/.aitools/config.json by the installer config step today and by setup-user-config after M3.
- source: scripts/aitools-install.sh/.ps1 (config step, today); reference/user-repo.md 'Config Schema (v3)' and scripts/setup-user-config.sh/.ps1 (after M3); decision G-recovered (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)
- Basis: `scripts/aitools-install.sh` sets `state = 'recovered'` and reports `write_summary WARN "aitools config" "rebuilt (was invalid)"` (around lines 451-509). The S5-Profile setup-user-config draft does the same.

#### verified (modified; G-verified, tree section 6)
- old definition: Deployment outcome: deployed content matches source. Active confirmation that the file is correct — no write needed.
- new definition: Deployment outcome: deployed content matches source. Active confirmation that the file is correct — no write needed. Separate meaning: the 'verified' field of a knownPaths entry in the tool registry ('YYYY-MM-DD (vX.Y.Z)') records empirical path verification per the script-standards rule 'KnownPaths empirical verification', not this deployment outcome; entries without it are unverified and ignored.
- old source: reference/managed-file-deployment.md
- new source: reference/managed-file-deployment.md; reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md section 6 (knownPaths field, after M2); decision G-verified

### Follow-up commit 61975fe: rule lines and the rest of the G-terms batch

The Session Commander approved this commit (open questions 1 and 2) and gave this mission ownership of `.claude/rules/glossary.md` for it.

#### `.claude/rules/glossary.md`

11 lines added to the first block of `### Terms`, in alphabetical position, at the same positions as the S4-Review R9 draft:

`aitools config`, `detection signature`, `install provenance`, `machine alias`, `machine id`, `machine profile`, `profile`, `recovered`, `tool adoption`, `tool exception`, `update channel`

#### `registries/glossary.json` (G-terms, approved "with the E-glossary-1 fix")

##### tool exception (new)
- definition: A recorded per-machine decision to keep a non-preferred or unknown-source install of a managed tool instead of replacing it, made with [k]eep in the install-method menu and stored in this machine's machine profile under toolExceptions ({decision: keep, detected: {platform, method, path, version}, decidedAt, decidedBy, reason}). While it matches the detected method and path, setup scripts report OK <version> (kept), and do not prompt, update, or check its PATH resolution; the minimum-version check still runs.
- source: reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md section 8 (semantics, after M2); reference/user-repo.md (storage shape); decisions N-Q1, D-TS10 (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)
- sourceDiscipline: Software asset management (exception register)

##### update channel (new)
- definition: The release stream a managed tool's preferred install follows, set per tool in the tool registry (updateChannel, default latest-stable; channel list in reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md 'Update channels'). Every installer run (aitools install, or aitools sync running the installer) runs the preferred method's update command for its channel and verifies the result; it does not query upstream for the newest version number.
- source: .claude/rules/tool-lifecycle.md; reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md section 4 (after M2); decisions D-TS5, T-Q6 (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)

##### machine profile (new; E-glossary-1 wording)
- definition: A field inside the profile (profile.json): one entry of its 'profiles' object, keyed by machine alias, holding one machine's (or one hosted environment's) per-machine overrides of identity name/company, machine id, and tool exceptions. Chosen on every run by the machine resolver (scripts/aitools-profile.sh/.ps1); never another machine's profile as a fallback. Not a separate file and not a shell profile (.zshrc, PowerShell $PROFILE).
- source: reference/user-repo.md (after M3); decisions G-profile, E-glossary-1, P-Q3 (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)

##### machine alias (new)
- definition: The key of a machine profile (for example nobul-mac). Chosen or registered at aitools user init (non-interactively with --alias) and cached per machine in ~/.aitools/config.json machineAlias, which is re-derived on every run. Not the base artifact 'alias' (a shell alias file).
- source: reference/user-repo.md (after M3); decision P-Q12 (plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md)

##### managed tool (modified)
- old definition: A CLI tool, dependency, or build tool governed by reference/tool-registry.md. Each gets setup scripts, platform lifecycle tracking, and a corresponding user skill. Standing orders and hooks enforce tool selection.
- new definition: A CLI tool, dependency, or build tool with an entry in the tool registry (/tool-registry skill). Each is installed through the tool support decision tree (a setup script, or an installer step that owns it inline), platform and environment lifecycle tracking, and a corresponding user skill. Standing orders and hooks enforce tool selection.
- old source: reference/framework-adoption.md
- new source: reference/framework-adoption.md; /tool-registry skill; reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md (after M2)

#### Departures from the S4-Review draft text (to match later decisions)

- **tool exception**: adds the N-Q1 storage shape and the D-TS10 "OK (kept)" report. It does not name a writer: N-Q1 says `aitools-profile.js` and P-Q3 says the resolver is native, so that is left to M3.
- **update channel**: says the update command is the mechanism, with no upstream version query (T-Q6).
- **machine profile**: uses the E-glossary-1 wording and the native resolver (P-Q3). It states that a machine profile is a field inside the profile (G-profile).
- **machine alias**: adds `--alias` (P-Q12).

The `/glossary` SKILL.md is not touched; its dead path belongs to LAYOUT.
