######## PR #92 (2026-10-23T01:29:08Z) M3 B8b: skills (ps1) and cursor use the one dotprofile resolver
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/92  +189/-30 files=4
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B8b/S3-M3-B8b-report.md, scripts/setup-user-cursor.ps1, scripts/setup-user-cursor.sh, scripts/setup-user-skills.ps1

## Summary

M3 B8b: `setup-user-skills.ps1` and `setup-user-cursor.sh/.ps1` get the dotprofile from the one resolver (`Resolve-UserRepoPath` / `resolve_user_repo_path`, PR #80). They no longer read `config.json` `userRepoPath` themselves. The cursor node block now receives the path and the resolver rc as arguments.

**Stacked on #80** (and, through it, #79, #77 and #76). This PR adds one commit of its own, 1554d80, plus a merge of origin/main.

**Merge with or after B5.** The generated `deploy/setup-user-cursor.sh` now calls the resolver, which is not embedded yet (see Follow-ups).

## Why

- **M3 B8b:** S5-Profile plan B8, M3 plan batch 2.
- **SD-7:** one dotprofile resolver.
- **Commander decision 3:** logging audit on the touched paths.

## Changes

Every touched path reads the rc and does not suppress it: 0 found, 1 shared-only (expected), 3 resolver error. On 3 the wrapper has already logged the error and written the ERROR row `dotprofile`. `resolve_user_repo_path` writes no ACTION row, and callers do not add one.

| File | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `setup-user-skills.ps1` | `ReadConfigKey config.json userRepoPath` | `$urpRc = Resolve-UserRepoPath`; copy `$script:AitLibUserRepoPath`. On 3: the skills deploy is **skipped** (6d4ccdf, S3-M3Lead decision): an error is logged and counted, ERROR row `skills | skipped (dotprofile error)`, no deploy and no reverse discovery, so shared copies never overwrite deployed dotprofile versions. rc 1 still deploys shared skills. B8a applies the same to `setup-user-skills.sh` |
| `setup-user-cursor.sh` | node read `~/.aitools/config.json` -> `cfg.userRepoPath` | bash calls `resolve_user_repo_path` before `node -e` and passes `"$USER_REPO_PATH" "$URP_RC"` as argv[4]/argv[5]. The node preferences block uses them. On rc 3 it prints the status `skipped-dotprofile` |
| `setup-user-cursor.ps1` | `config.json` read inside the preferences block | `Resolve-UserRepoPath` inside the preferences block (the block is replaced in deploy/). Sets `$prefsUnresolved`, which is defined just before the block so the deploy copy has it too |

**rc 3 in cursor.** The `cli-config.json` merge is **skipped** rather than writing the default preferences (vimMode false) over the user's choices. The run gets ERROR row `cursor cli | skipped (dotprofile error)` and no second error count. Every code path still writes a summary row.

**Logging audit (touched paths):**
- **cursor.sh:** an unreadable or corrupt `profile.json` used to go to node **stderr**: never logged, never counted. It is now a `WARN:` line, which becomes `log_warn`. A missing `profile.json` with a resolved dotprofile is now a `DETAIL:` line.
- **cursor.ps1:**
  - `Get-Content -ErrorAction Stop` inside the try.
  - A missing `profile.json` is a `LogDetail`.
  - `vimMode` and `model` are taken only when they are bool and string, matching the .sh `typeof` checks. Before this, a string `"false"` became `$true` through `[bool]`.
- **skills.ps1:** nothing else on the touched path suppresses errors.

**Markers:** the build-deploy extraction markers are unchanged.

######## PR #93 (2026-10-23T01:57:36Z) MDM: build-deploy.sh generates an embedded deploy/deploy-harness.* and ports the Cursor fan-out headers
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/93  +1337/-13 files=6
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H-MDM/S3-H-MDM-Review-reverify.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H-MDM/S3-H-MDM-Review-verdict.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H-MDM/S3-H-MDM-drafts/dot-claude/rules/deploy-paths.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H-MDM/S3-H-MDM-handback.md, scripts/build-deploy.sh, tests/deploy-harness-mdm/test-deploy-harness-mdm.sh

Merge after #88 and #87; diff shrinks to build-deploy.sh once they merge. (This branch merges both, so until then the diff also shows their files.)

## Summary

- `scripts/build-deploy.sh` now generates `<dotprofile>/deploy/deploy-harness.sh` and `.ps1`, the MDM path of `scripts/deploy-harness.*` (#88). They are self-contained: every harness source file is embedded at build time, so no repo, dotprofile checkout or git is needed at run time.
- Equivalence was tested under isolated HOMEs, with real repo content, for .sh and .ps1. The dev path and the MDM path produce byte-identical `~/.aitools/{reference,registries,hooks,rules,skills,bin}`, the same exec bits, config.json, summary rows and exit codes (132 added each).
- The `setup-user-cursor` MDM headers are now extracted from the source headers. This picks up step 4 and the `~/.cursor/rules` scope from #87.
- `deploy/` now also gets the config.json writer that deploy-harness calls: `setup-user-config`, `aitools-profile` and `aitools-json`, copied as-is with the library inlined.

## Why

`.claude/rules/deploy-paths.md`: the dev and MDM paths must produce equivalent results. The S3-Harness brief says "MDM: build-deploy.sh generates an embedded deploy/deploy-harness.* (isolated HOME only)". S3-H-Core's open question 3 suggested embedding the file list at build time, because the endpoint has no git checkout.

## Changes

**Snapshot, not a second copy (I6).**
- For each source directory, the build records `git ls-files -z` and the listed files with their exec bits, plus the bin CLIs.
- The directories come from the dev script's own `add_source` calls and the CLIs from its `for cli in` line, so nothing is listed twice.
- The build checks parity against the `.ps1` `Add-Source` count and bin list.

**Generated script = the dev code plus one replaced section.**
- From `scripts/deploy-harness.*` it extracts the preamble, flags and section 1 (config.json), then sections 3-7.
- The cut points are the existing section headings, via the new `extract_span`: each heading must match exactly one line, or the build fails.
- Only section 2 (the dotprofile lookup) is replaced. The replacement unpacks the base64 payload to a temp directory laid out like the repos. Each file gets a `# path (bytes, sha256)` comment, so deploy/ diffs stay readable.
- A `git` function answers the plan's two git calls (`rev-parse --is-inside-work-tree`, `ls-files -z`) for snapshot directories only. Every other call goes to the real git.
- The adopt target is `""` (`replace_once`, exactly one occurrence), the same as for the embedded skills: the source is the embed, not a repo file.
- PS1 removes its temp directory in `finally`. This was probed on pwsh: the block runs on `exit`, including `exit 2` from a nested function.

**Non-git dotprofile at build time.** Pre-push step 7 builds into a `git archive` copy. In that case the build lists the files on disk in git's order, with a warning. The test asserts the output is identical to a git build.

**Cursor headers.** The hand-written "Does three things" blocks are replaced by `extract_span` from the source headers. A line says to run deploy-harness first.

**New test.** `tests/deploy-harness-mdm/test-deploy-harness-mdm.sh` covers 26 cases.

## Verification

All runs were on macOS with isolated HOMEs. The real HOME and the real dotprofile were never written to.

- Independent review (S3-H-MDM-Review):
  - First pass: CHANGES REQUESTED, 3 blocking (https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/93#pullrequestreview-5621367406).
  - Fixed in `3e1a97d`: every embedded block is checked against its size and sha256, block counts are checked, and a truncated .sh fails; exec bits come from git modes; perl paths are passed as arguments for Windows; the .ps1 source list is compared with the .sh list; the managed scope is in the header; the build warns about uncommitted changes.
  - Re-verify: NO BLOCKING FINDINGS (https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/93#pullrequestreview-5621439226).
- Fresh endpoint without the installer: deploy-harness exits 1 because config.json cannot be generated without the installer's bootstrap keys (`reposPath`, `repoPath`). This is now documented in the headers and the draft. Whether MDM should skip the config writer in that case is left to the commander (hand-back, open question 1).
- `bash tests/deploy-harness-mdm/test-deploy-harness-mdm.sh`: **29 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. It adds three cases: a corrupted block (.sh and .ps1) and a truncated script, each ending in ERROR and exit 1. It covers:

  - on a pty, the menu offers `[o]verwrite` and no adopt or merge
  - `--help`; non-git dotprofile build produces identical output
  - the .ps1 pair on pwsh, with OS guards disabled in temp copies
- A scratch run on real repo content plus a `git archive` copy of the dotprofile: `OK|harness files|132 added` on both paths. All 6 directories, exec bits (20), config.json and summary rows are identical. The same holds for .ps1 (normal and `-DryRun`), and no temp directories were left behind.

######## PR #95 (2026-10-23T01:23:09Z) M3 B5: deploy/ resolves the dotprofile and machine profile on the target machine
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/95  +2441/-671 files=26
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B5a-Review/S3-M3-B5a-Review-pr95.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B5a/S3-M3-B5a-drafts/dot-claude/rules/deploy-paths.md.edit.txt, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B5a/S3-M3-B5a-report.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B8a/S3-M3-B8a-Review-pr91.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B8a/S3-M3-B8a-report.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B8b/S3-M3-B8b-report.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B8c/S3-M3-B8c-Review-pr90.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-B8c/S3-M3-B8c-report.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-Claude-Review/S3-M3-Claude-Review-pr83.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-M3-Claude/S3-M3-Claude-report.md, scripts/ait-harvest.py, scripts/build-deploy.sh, scripts/check-lib.ps1, scripts/check-lib.sh, scripts/check-post-push.ps1, scripts/check-post-push.sh, scripts/check-pre-push.ps1, scripts/check-pre-push.sh, scripts/setup-user-claude.ps1, scripts/setup-user-claude.sh, scripts/setup-user-cursor.ps1, scripts/setup-user-cursor.sh, scripts/setup-user-settings.ps1, scripts/setup-user-settings.sh, scripts/setup-user-skills.ps1, scripts/setup-user-skills.sh

## Summary

M3 B5, part a: the generated `deploy/` scripts now resolve the dotprofile and this machine's profile **on the target machine**, the way `scripts/` does. No machine's values are interpolated at build time any more.

- **Stacked on #83 + #90 / #91 / #92.** This branch is #83's branch with the heads of #91 (B8a), #92 (B8b) and #90 (B8c) merged in, so every switched consumer is present. Merge it after them or together with them. #91 and #92 must not merge without this PR: without it the generated `deploy/setup-user-settings.sh` and `deploy/setup-user-cursor.sh` exit 1 with `ERROR|dotprofile|resolver missing`.
- This PR's own commits: c421511 and 266a1ba.

## Why

- **The cross-machine defect (B5, decisions 3-4).** `build-deploy.sh` picked one profile at build time (`machineAlias`, else hostname, else the first profile) and wrote its values into the template. `deploy/` is committed once and used by every machine of the user, so every machine got that one machine's name and company. Negative control on this branch's base: with a two-profile fixture, the old build logged `Profile interpolation: name=One & /…/home \1` (profile m1, and `$HOME` expanded by the `eval`), whatever the target machine.
- **B8a INCIDENT.** The switched consumers call the lib's resolver wrappers, but `deploy/` carried no resolver.
- **Review H1, H5, M7, L8; SD-7.**
  - H1: the inliners replaced every line that names the lib.
  - H5: step 7 had to pass its temp copy explicitly.
  - M7: step 7's trigger missed the resolver.
  - L8 / SD-7: build-deploy read config.json itself.

## Changes

**`scripts/build-deploy.sh`**
- **Dotprofile from the entry point.** The build takes `AITOOLS_USER_REPO_PATH`. A standalone run asks `resolve_user_repo_path` (SD-7) in a child bash; only the path comes back, on fd 3. No config.json read (L8).
  - rc 1 (no dotprofile) and rc 3 (resolver error) skip the build with exit 0, as before (H3: a failing prebuild would block setup-user-config).
  - A corrupt `profile.json` fails loud (exit 1).
- **No build-time interpolation.** Removed: the hardcoded `Jose` / `Nobul` / `jose@nobul.tech` defaults, the node alias/hostname/first-profile lookup and its `eval`. JSON reads use `aitools-json.pl` (core perl, every platform).
- **`setup-user-claude.sh/.ps1` are now pure extract** of `scripts/` (interpolation, footer, managed-file deploy, rules logic). This removes this pair's I6 duplication. build-deploy adds two generated parts:
  - a profile/template source block: the live dotprofile on the machine wins (what `scripts/` reads); else the build-time **profile snapshot** (`aitools-json.pl snapshot`), **template** (the dotprofile's `claude/CLAUDE.md`, else `shared/claude-shared.md`; tokens left in place) and **rules**. A dotprofile error (rc 3) ends the run with the outcome `scripts/` gives it.
  - a rules fallback between the rules source selection and the rules logic.
- **Embedded resolver.** `aitools-profile.sh` / `aitools-lib.sh` / `aitools-json.pl` and the three `.ps1` counterparts are copied from `scripts/` at build time into `setup-user-claude`, `-settings` and `-cursor`. Being copies, the re-key (#86) flows through on the next build.
  - At runtime they are written to a temp dir and `AITOOLS_PROFILE_SH` / `AITOOLS_PROFILE_PS1` point the lib at it; a caller-set value wins.
  - The resolver's own dependencies (`$SCRIPT_DIR/…`, `Join-Path $PSScriptRoot "…"`) are checked against the embed list. A missing one fails the build, not an endpoint.
  - bash: one quoted heredoc per file; a file holding the delimiter line fails the build. PowerShell: one here-string per file with every line prefixed `|`, so no line can end it (`'@`).
  - Cleanup: bash by an EXIT trap. PowerShell by `Remove-AitoolsEmbed` before the exit footer, which also restores both env vars. An early `exit` in PowerShell leaves the temp dir behind (PowerShell has no EXIT trap; `PowerShell.Exiting` does not fire under `-File`, tested).
- **H1, inliners.** The embed block is inserted after the lib, outside the pattern scan. The inliners now replace only the **first** line that loads the lib (`^source …aitools-lib.sh` / `^. …aitools-lib.ps1`), and fail the build when there is none.
  - This also fixes a live bug: `deploy/setup-user-skills.sh` replaced the aitool-ops SKILL.md line "Hooks cannot source `aitools-lib.sh` …" with the whole lib, so the deployed skill held ~2,700 lines of lib.
- **`setup-user-cursor.sh/.ps1`.** The whole body is extracted. Only the two preference defaults in the profile preferences block become the build-time `cursor.cli` values, so the live `profile.json` wins on a machine with the dotprofile. A non-plain model id (it becomes code) is not embedded: warning, fallback `auto`.
- **Order and invariants.** `deploy/` is reset only after every input was read and checked: a failing build keeps the committed `deploy/`. Post-build, every resolver consumer must hold the embedded resolver exactly once.

**`scripts/check-pre-push.sh` / `.ps1`, step 7 only**
- **H5.** The build gets `AITOOLS_USER_REPO_PATH=<temp copy>` and no `AITOOLS_PROFILE_FILE` / `AITOOLS_MACHINE_ALIAS` / `AITOOLS_SUMMARY_FILE`.
- **M7.** The trigger includes `scripts/aitools-profile.(sh|ps1)` and `scripts/aitools-json.(pl|ps1)`.
- **Smoke run (B8a INCIDENT).** After the build, the generated `setup-user-claude`, `-settings` and `-cursor` run with `--dry-run` (`-DryRun`) under the isolated HOME. The step fails on exit > 1, on "resolver missing", or when claude logs no `Profile source:` line.
  - Why step 7 and not build-deploy: build-deploy stays a pure generator that never executes what it generates. It also runs inside `aitools` on every run, where a smoke run would be slow and would touch the machine. Step 7 already owns "the built deploy/ is right".
- **Skipped builds.** The copy's committed `deploy/` is removed before the build, so a build that skips now fails the step instead of passing the compare.
- **Log pointer.** Failure messages say "see deploy.log": step details go there (`check_log_init`), not to checks.log.

## Verification

macOS. Every run used an isolated HOME and a fixture dotprofile copy. Two machine profiles: m1 (`id-m1`) and m2 (`id-m2`); `AITOOLS_PROFILE_TEST_MACHINE_ID` / `_HOSTNAME` select the machine. Never the real HOME, never the live dotprofile. Harness: `.scratch/session-B5a/` (not committed).

- **`verify.sh`: 71/0 under bash 5 and 71/0 under /bin/bash 3.2** (build and scripts under that bash).
  - **Cross-machine, with the dotprofile:** for m1 and for m2, `deploy/setup-user-claude.sh` gives a CLAUDE.md byte-identical to `scripts/setup-user-claude.sh`, with identical rules and summary rows. Each machine gets its own name/company and footer `profile m1|m2`, and none of the other machine's values.
  - **Cross-machine, without the dotprofile** (deploy/ copied to an endpoint, `config.json` `{}`): each machine resolves its own profile from the embedded snapshot, with rules from the embedded copy and no ERROR rows. An unregistered machine gets identity values only and `ERROR|machine profile`, never another machine's values.
  - **rc 3 parity:** `deploy/` and `scripts/` give the same summary rows and exit 1, and the personal CLAUDE.md is kept.

  - **Other:** the endpoint's cursor uses the build-time fallback; the temp dirs are removed; a dry run writes nothing.
- **`edge.sh`: 26/0.** Cases:
  - no dotprofile → skip;

  - claude endpoint m1/m2 from the snapshot, and live profile with the dotprofile;

- **`step7-test.sh`: 8/0**, against the real `check-pre-push.sh` with a fixture:

  - **H5:** with `AITOOLS_USER_REPO_PATH` / `AITOOLS_PROFILE_FILE` / `AITOOLS_MACHINE_ALIAS` exported to a sentinel dotprofile, the sentinel's deploy/ and git tree stay untouched;
  - a stale committed file → FAIL naming it.
- **Repo suites:**

  - test-config 89/0;
  - test-lib 108/0 (/bin/bash 3.2 `--bash-only` 44/0);
  - tests/logging: sh 24/0, ps 15/0 (5 skipped);
  - managed-files sh 42/0.
- **Syntax:**
  - `bash -n` on build-deploy.sh and check-pre-push.sh;

  - the build's own ParseFile pass: all 21 generated `.ps1` clean.
- **check-pre-commit:** step 6 FAIL (known: harvesting file mode). WARNs: 3 (build freshness), 5, 9, and 11 (the real dotprofile has uncommitted `deploy/` changes dated 14:46 local, before this mission's first build; they hold no B5 marker, so they are not from this mission).
- **check-pre-push:** step 7 FAIL "stale" (known; the real dotprofile's committed deploy/ predates this branch). Its smoke run against a copy of the real dotprofile passed: claude `Profile source: live …`, all three `COMPLETED successfully`. ######## PR #98 (2026-10-27T00:38:04Z) setup-user-config: add --no-machine / -NoMachine (no duplicate machine advice)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/98  +435/-10 files=5
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-NoMachine/S3-MF-NoMachine-Review-pr98.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-NoMachine/S3-MF-NoMachine-report.md, scripts/setup-user-config.ps1, scripts/setup-user-config.sh, tests/profile/test-config.sh

## Summary

`setup-user-config.sh` / `.ps1` gain `--no-machine` / `-NoMachine`. When `aitools user init` leaves this machine unregistered, it can pass the flag, and the config writer then adds no machine-profile ERROR or ACTION of its own. The run shows the advice once, from user init.

## Why

M3-FINISH item 2 (commander, 2026-10-22): "setup-user-config gains `--no-machine` / `-NoMachine`: when user init leaves the machine unregistered, the writer adds no ERROR/ACTION of its own (no duplicates)." This is the open review finding m2 from S3-M3-UserInit (PR #86, open question 1).

## Changes

- `scripts/setup-user-config.sh`, `scripts/setup-user-config.ps1`, with the same behaviour and the same summary rows in both:
  - A new flag: `--no-machine` (bash) / `-NoMachine` (PowerShell switch).
  - Resolver errors go through `derive_error` / `Write-DeriveError`. With the flag, "no profile matches this machine" and "profile.json has no machine profiles" are logged at detail only. Every other error is still `log_error` / `LogError`.
  - Resolver warnings that name `run: aitools user init` or `run: aitools-profile migrate` are logged at detail with the flag. They are not counted, and no `NEED_*` flag is set, so no section-3 ACTION is written.
  - With the flag, the section-1 "no machine match" ERROR and ACTION rows are skipped, but only when the unregistered errors were the only errors.
  - With the flag, the merge never sets or removes `machineId` / `machineAlias`.
  - The usage line, `--help` / `-Help` and the header comment are updated, including the managed/preserved fields note.
  - The PS1 `$profileBad` variable became `$script:ProfileBad`, because the new function sets it.
- `tests/profile/test-config.sh`:
  - New cases c22 to c22j. Each case runs both writers, checks that the config.json bytes and the summary rows are identical, and checks the exit codes.
  - `map_args` maps `--no-machine` to `-NoMachine`.

## Contract change

This adds to the writer contract from PR #86 (S3-M3Lead plan 3b). Nothing in the existing contract changes: `--machine-id` / `-MachineId`, the deprecated `--machine-alias` / `-MachineAlias` (one release), exit codes 0/1 (never 2), no prompts, env and dry-run behaviour, and at most one ACTION row.

`--no-machine` / `-NoMachine` means: the caller owns machine registration and reports it.

- **Written:** every other managed field (`version`, `meta`, `reposPath`, `repoPath`, `googleDrives`, `githubUser`, `userRepoPath`), derived as usual.
- **Preserved:** `machineId` and `machineAlias` stay exactly as they are in config.json. They are not set and not deleted. A stale `machineId` that a normal run would remove (c20) is kept (c22e). A value salvaged from a corrupt file is still salvaged.
- **Not reported** for an unregistered machine ("no profile matches this machine", "profile.json has no machine profiles"):
  - no `ERROR|machine profile|no machine match` row and no `ACTION||Run: aitools user init (profiles: ...)` row;
  - no section-3 ACTION ("registers this machine", "re-keys machine profiles", "updates this machine's profile");
  - those resolver messages go to deploy.log at `[detail]`.
- **Exit code:** an unregistered machine alone gives exit 0.
- **Still reported** (exit 1): an invalid profile.json (`ERROR|profile.json|invalid (see log)`), a missing dotprofile path, ambiguous matches (hostname or machine.id on more than one profile), and bootstrap or validation failures.
- **Log:** one info line, `config: --no-machine: machine registration is left to the caller -- ...`. PowerShell writes `-NoMachine` in that line.
- **Bad arguments:** the flag together with `--machine-id` / `-MachineId` or `--machine-alias` / `-MachineAlias` gives `ERROR|aitools config|bad arguments`, exit 1, and nothing is written.

## Verification (macOS, Darwin arm64)

- `bash -n` (bash 5 and `/bin/bash` 3.2) on `setup-user-config.sh` and `test-config.sh`: clean. The pwsh parser on `setup-user-config.ps1`: clean.
- `bash tests/profile/test-config.sh` (pwsh parity): **258 passed, 0 failed**.
- `AITOOLS_TEST_BASH=/bin/bash bash tests/profile/test-config.sh --bash-only`: **167 passed, 0 failed**.
- `bash tests/profile/test-user-init.sh`: **200 passed, 0 failed**. The caller is unchanged; this run shows nothing regressed.

- `bash scripts/check-pre-push.sh`, run under an isolated HOME:
  - Without a userRepoPath: 0 FAIL.
  - With userRepoPath pointing at the dotprofile, so step 7 runs on a `git archive` temp copy: 1 FAIL, step 7. This is the known pre-existing stale dotprofile `deploy/` (every generated file is listed, not only this writer).

## Not run

- Windows / Git Bash. On macOS the PowerShell writer ran under pwsh through `AITOOLS_PROFILE_TEST_ANY_OS=1`.
- `build-deploy.sh` into the real dotprofile (by design).
- An end-to-end `aitools user init` that passes the flag. That is S3-MF-Runner's change.

######## PR #100 (2026-10-27T03:32:35Z) deploy-harness: write a rules source manifest and retire removed rules
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/100  +774/-4 files=7
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/dot-claude/rules/managed-file-deployment.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/reference/harness.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/reference/managed-file-deployment.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-handback.md, scripts/deploy-harness.ps1, scripts/deploy-harness.sh, tests/deploy-harness/test-deploy-harness.sh

## Summary

deploy-harness.sh/.ps1 now write a per-file source manifest,
`~/.aitools/rules/.aitools-rules-manifest`, on every run, and retire rules
whose source was removed: the deployed copy is backed up and removed, and
the manifest keeps a `retired` line for the consumers. Mission
S3-H2-Manifest (HARNESS-2).

## Why

Approved HARNESS Q2 (S3-Harness handback): a removed source rule stayed in
`~/.aitools/rules` and in the tools' rule directories, and stayed loaded.
The manifest gives the fan-out consumers (setup-user-claude,
setup-user-cursor; missions S3-H2-Claude / S3-H2-Cursor) each rule's
source, so Cursor can take user-level rules only, and every consumer can
retire what was removed.

## Protected-file drafts awaiting approval

- `S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/reference/harness.md` -- new subsection "Rules
  manifest and retired rules"; "No deletion" bullet amended.
- `S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/dot-claude/rules/managed-file-deployment.md` --
  Content sources "Rules" bullet.
- `S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/reference/managed-file-deployment.md` -- Content
  Sources "User rules" row; script-owned text paragraph.

## Contract change

Four changes to the shared contract, each for safety or determinism:

1. **Only a listed source retires.** The launch contract retired any name
   "absent from every source this run". A dotprofile that is not
   configured, not found, or not a git checkout would then retire, and
   delete, every user rule on that run (and `.claude/rules` if the repo
   listing failed). Now a name whose previous source was not listed this
   run keeps its previous line and its file.
2. **A retired name's file is removed on every run while it exists**, not
   only on the run that retires it. This makes a failed backup or removal
   self-healing, and keeps `~/.aitools/rules` consistent with the
   manifest. Each removal is backed up first; a failed backup leaves the
   file in place (log_error + ERROR row).
3. **Names are compared case-insensitively** (as the conflict check is):
   `Scratch.md` in the previous manifest and `scratch.md` in a source are
   the same rule. Without this, removing the "retired" `Scratch.md` on a
   case-insensitive file system would delete the live `scratch.md`.
4. **Added**: unknown header -> warning, nothing retired, rewritten;
   invalid lines (not `*.md`, `*.bak.*`, `/` or `\` in the name, unknown
   source) -> ignored with a warning (no path outside `~/.aitools/rules`
   is ever touched); dry run -> logged only.

## Contract

- File: `~/.aitools/rules/.aitools-rules-manifest` (no .md extension, so consumers that copy only *.md never copy it).
- Format: UTF-8, LF, first line `# aitools rules manifest v1`, then one line per rule: `<file name><TAB><source>`, source one of `shared` (aitools shared/rules), `aitools` (aitools .claude/rules), `dotprofile` (dotprofile claude/rules), `retired` (was in a previous manifest, no longer in any source). Lines sorted by file name (byte order). Only *.md file names; never *.bak.*.
- deploy-harness writes it on every run after the rules copy (temp file then move), recording the source that won (first source wins; duplicates already produce log_error + ERROR row).
- Retiring: a name present in the previous manifest (with a non-retired source) and absent from every source this run (names compared case-insensitively) becomes `retired`, but only when its previous source was listed this run; a name whose source was not listed (directory not found, not a git work tree, git ls-files failed, no dotprofile configured) keeps its previous line and file. While a name is `retired`, deploy-harness backs up and removes `~/.aitools/rules/<name>` whenever it exists (a failed backup leaves it: log_error + ERROR row), logs it, and keeps the `retired` line in later manifests until the name reappears in a source. With no previous manifest (first run) nothing is retired. A previous manifest with another first line: warning, nothing retired, rewritten. Invalid lines: ignored with a warning.
- Consumers: take every non-retired name they are entitled to; back up and remove their own deployed copy of every `retired` name (Cursor also removes names whose source is `aitools`).
- Manifest write failure: log_error + ERROR row, run continues.

## Follow-ups

- Consumers: S3-H2-Claude, S3-H2-Cursor read the manifest. Note for
  setup-user-cursor.ps1: its `Get-ChildItem` lists the dot file and logs
  "Ignoring non-rule file in harness rules: .aitools-rules-manifest" on
  every run (the .sh glob skips dot files); the Cursor mission may want to
  skip it silently.
- S3-H2-MDM: when adding body markers, section 5b must stay inside the
  extracted span.
- Pre-existing divergence (not changed here): the rules filter is
  case-sensitive in the .sh (`*.md`, `*.bak.*`) and case-insensitive in the
  .ps1 (`-like`), so `X.MD` deploys on Windows only.
- Review parity notes (low, open): the .ps1 retire backups are never
  pruned (the .sh backup_file keeps 20); on a failed backup the .sh counts
  a warning plus an error, the .ps1 only the error; a dangling symlink with
  a retired name is removed by the .sh, left by the .ps1.
- `Backup-File` in aitools-lib.ps1 resolves wildcards (`[ ]` names): no
  backup, success reported. deploy-harness.ps1 no longer uses it for
  retirement (commit 161edd0); the library fix belongs to its owner.
- A machine that runs both the dev path and an older MDM deploy/ would
  retire and re-add rules between the two snapshots (each run is correct
  for its own sources).

Behaviour note: the old PS1 block ran `git commit` without a pathspec, so it committed everything staged in the dotprofile; the old bash block discarded git's stderr. The summary row for a failed push changes from `WARN|claude rules|<rule> not pushed` to a warning + ACTION `run git push in <dotprofile>` (as user init does).
- `tests/managed-files/test-adopt-push.sh` / `.ps1` (new) and three CI jobs in `.github/workflows/check.yml`.

## Contract

`dotprofile_commit_push FILE MESSAGE` (bash) / `Invoke-DotprofileCommitPush -File FILE -Message MESSAGE` (PS1)

- In the dotprofile = FILE's git top level equals the dotprofile's git top level. Dotprofile = `AITLIB_USER_REPO_PATH` / `$script:AitLibUserRepoPath` when the caller ran the resolver (set, even to `""`), else a quiet resolver call (no ERROR row).
- Pathspec commit: `git add -- FILE`, `git commit -m MESSAGE -- FILE`. Push: `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, ssh BatchMode unless `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` is set. git output -> deploy.log detail.
- Result global `AITLIB_DOTPROFILE_PUSH` / `$script:AitLibDotprofilePush`:
  - `committed-pushed`: ok line
  - `unchanged`: file matches HEAD; no commit, no push, no warning
  - `commit-failed`: warn + ACTION `commit and push <file> in <dotprofile>` (also when the dotprofile is not a git repository)
  - `push-failed`: warn + ACTION `git pull --rebase, then git push in <dotprofile>` (rejected / non-fast-forward / fetch first) or `run git push in <dotprofile>`
  - `not-dotprofile`: detail line only
- Always returns 0 (bash) / writes nothing to the output stream (PS1, it runs inside `Deploy-ManagedFile`). ACTIONs once per run. The deploy result stays `accept & adopt`.
- Adopt-path commit message: `Adopt <file name> from the deployed copy`.

## Verification (macOS, this machine)

- `bash tests/managed-files/test-adopt-push.sh`: 28 passed, 0 failed (adopt/merge/JSON adopt into a temp dotprofile with a bare remote -> committed + pushed, commit holds that file only; unrelated unstaged and staged changes not committed; push failure -> WARN + ACTION, outcome still accept & adopt, exit 0; rejected push -> pull --rebase ACTION; commit failure (pre-commit hook) -> WARN + ACTION; two failures -> one ACTION; adopt outside the dotprofile -> no commit; resolver fallback for an unresolved caller, as deploy-harness)
- `pwsh -NoProfile -File tests/managed-files/test-adopt-push.ps1`: 23 passed, 0 failed
- `bash tests/managed-files/test-managed-files.sh`: 57 passed, 0 failed (no regression)
- `pwsh -NoProfile -File tests/managed-files/test-managed-files.ps1`: 39 passed, 0 failed
- `bash -n` and the pwsh parser on every touched script: OK
- `bash scripts/check-pre-commit.sh`: 12 PASS, 6 WARN, 1 FAIL (step 6: the known `harvesting/2026-07-09_session-ba4c0c12-7_build-deploy-prefix.sh` 100644)
- `bash scripts/check-pre-push.sh`: 6 PASS, 3 WARN, 1 FAIL (step 7: the known stale dotprofile `deploy/`)

- Linux: CI only.

######## PR #106 (2026-10-27T05:54:39Z) Run deploy-harness from the installer and every aitools run
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/106  +834/-103 files=13
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-AbortDocs/S3-AbortDocs-handback.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Hookin/S3-H2-Hookin-handback.md, .claude/rules/interactive-menus.md, .cursor/rules/interactive-menus.mdc, plans/tool-support-and-machine-profile.md, reference/managed-file-deployment.md, reference/tool-support-decision-tree.md, scripts/aitools, scripts/aitools-install.ps1, scripts/aitools-install.sh, scripts/aitools.ps1, tests/hookin/hookin-driver.ps1, tests/hookin/test-hookin.sh

## Summary

`deploy-harness` now runs in every `aitools install` and every `aitools` sync, on both platforms. The installer's own `~/.aitools/bin` copy step is gone.

- **Installer** (`aitools-install.sh` / `.ps1`): a new Step 8b runs right after Step 8 (Node). It calls `run_harness_step` / `Invoke-HarnessStep`, which use the lib relay helpers `run_child_relayed` / `Invoke-ChildRelayed`.
- **Sync** (`scripts/aitools` / `aitools.ps1`): `deploy-harness` is now the first entry in the `deploy_configs` / `Deploy-Configs` list. `setup-user-config` is no longer in the list, because `deploy-harness` runs that writer itself.
- **Exit codes**:
  - 2 = the user chose `[x]abort` at a review menu. The run stops with exit 2 and the summary shows `WARN|harness files|aborted by user`.
  - 1 = errors. The summary shows `ERROR|harness files|script failed (exit 1)` and the run continues.

## Why

Mission S3-H2-Hookin (HARNESS-2). It follows the constraints in the S3-H-Core and S3-Harness hand-backs:
- the writer must not run twice per sync;
- `deploy-harness`'s stdin is never piped;
- only exit 2 means abort (D-TS13 as decided, no marker file), and the abort ends the whole run (T-Q3 / SD-5);
- Step 8b comes after Node;
- the installer's bin step is removed.

**Writer once per sync, and why the writer leaves the loop.** `deploy-harness` already calls the writer bare, or with `--dry-run` / `-DryRun` when `AITOOLS_DRY_RUN` is set (scripts/deploy-harness.sh:99-115, deploy-harness.ps1:89-108). The loop called it bare too (scripts/aitools:1151+1172 and aitools.ps1:157+184 on main). So I removed it from the loop and did not touch `deploy-harness`, which S3-H2-Manifest owns.

## Changes

- **`scripts/aitools-install.sh` / `.ps1`**
  - Step 8b runs `deploy-harness`. The bash side runs `bash -n` first, because bash exits 2 on a syntax error and that would read as an abort.
  - The old bin step and its `harness bin` row are removed. `deploy-harness` deploys the same three CLIs and sets their executable bit.
  - The installer exports `AITOOLS_RUN_ID`, so its own `deploy.jsonl` records and the lines relayed from `deploy-harness` share one run id.
  - The header comment now says the installer deploys the harness.
- **`scripts/aitools`**
  - New list order, plus `deploy_abort`. A `deploy_aborted` flag skips the remaining scripts and the AGENTS.md sync.
  - The call sites now `show_summary` and `return 2` on abort, and on installer exit 2.
  - macOS/Linux: `deploy-harness` runs relayed.
  - Git Bash on Windows: `deploy-harness.ps1` runs with plain `pwsh -File`. The PS1 review prompt is written to stdout with no newline, so a line-by-line relay would hold it back until the user answered.
- **`scripts/aitools.ps1`**: the same changes. `deploy-harness` runs in-process through `Invoke-ChildRelayed`, and `Invoke-DeployAbort` handles the abort.
- **`tests/hookin/test-hookin.sh` + `hookin-driver.ps1`**
  - Each case builds a fixture git repo with copies of the real scripts and runs under an isolated HOME.
  - `claude` and `node` are stubs on PATH, so nothing gets installed or updated.
  - Each run uses the real `deploy-harness`. The PS1 copy has its Windows guard disabled.
  - Abort is tested through a pty (`tests/managed-files/pty-run.py`).
  - The PS1 functions are loaded from the real files through the AST.

## Verification (macOS; pwsh 7 for the PS1 functions)

- `bash tests/hookin/test-hookin.sh`: **38 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. The suite checks:
  - `deploy-harness` runs once and the writer runs once per sync, on both platforms;
  - bin is deployed by `deploy-harness`, and there is no `harness bin` row;
  - Step 8b sits between Step 8 and Step 9;
  - `deploy.jsonl` has one run id;
  - exit 1 → ERROR row and the run continues;
  - abort → exit 2 (installer), or the flag set with no later script run (loop).
- `tests/deploy-harness/test-deploy-harness.sh`: 45/45.
- `tests/logging`: sh 24/24; ps1 15 passed, 5 skipped (Windows-only).
- `tests/profile/test-config.sh`: 190/190.
- `tests/profile/test-user-init.sh`: 200/200.
- `bash -n` and the pwsh parser pass on all touched scripts.
- `check-pre-commit`: the only FAIL is the known step 6 (harvesting file mode). The WARNs are build freshness, release notes, and the dotprofile having uncommitted changes (not mine). `check-pre-push`: 0 FAIL.

## Not run

- Either installer or `aitools` end to end against a real HOME.
- Any PS1 on Windows.
- The Git-Bash-on-Windows branch of `scripts/aitools`.
- The `aitools` top-level call sites of the new exit-2 handling. They were checked by reading only: the loop and installer functions are tested, the `main` wiring is not.
- `build-deploy.sh` (no change to the files it embeds).

## Protected-file drafts awaiting approval

None. One spec line no longer matches the code (follow-up 3), but I did not draft a change to it.

## Follow-ups / open questions

1. **`aitools install` still runs the writer twice.** It runs at Step 5 with the bootstrap flags, which `deploy-harness`'s writer needs, and again bare inside Step 8b, which normally verifies and writes nothing. Suggested fix: `deploy-harness` gets a `--skip-config` / `-SkipConfig` flag (S3-H2-Manifest owns it), and Step 8b passes it.
2. **The lib's relay logs an abort as an error.** `run_child_relayed` / `Invoke-ChildRelayed` log `"<label> failed (exit 2)"` at error level before the caller can see that it was an abort. That error is counted, on top of `deploy-harness`'s own `Aborted by user` error. Suggested fix: an abort-aware lib change (aitools-lib is out of this mission's scope).
3. **`reference/managed-file-deployment.md` "[x]abort" disagrees with the code.** It says the installer continues with the next script after an abort. T-Q3/SD-5 say abort ends the whole run, and this PR implements that. Only `deploy-harness`'s exit 2 aborts the run. Should the other loop scripts' exit 2 also end the run? Suggested answer: yes, per T-Q3. That would be a follow-up PR plus a spec edit.
4. **An abort skips the rest of the run.** That includes the `aitools` self-update re-stamp of the installed version and the relay-outbound prompt. This is intended.
5. **PR #100 changes `deploy-harness`.** If it adds files under `~/.aitools`, the "7 added" counts in `tests/hookin` need updating after it merges.

######## PR #108 (2026-10-27T00:58:51Z) Fix resolver and JSON engine audit findings A-6..A-9
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/108  +371/-35 files=8
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Fix6/S3-MF-Fix6-Review-pr108.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Fix6/S3-MF-Fix6-drafts/reference/script-standards-detail.md.edit.txt, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Fix6/S3-MF-Fix6-report.md, scripts/aitools-json.pl, scripts/aitools-json.ps1, scripts/aitools-profile.ps1, scripts/aitools-profile.sh, tests/profile/test-profile.sh

## Summary

Fixes S3-MF-Audit findings A-6 (code part), A-7, A-8 and A-9 in the profile resolver (`scripts/aitools-profile.sh` / `.ps1`) and the JSON engines (`scripts/aitools-json.pl` / `.ps1`). The resolver's output contract (`result`/`via`/`warn`/`error` lines, exit codes 0-4) is unchanged. The A-6 spec part (I-3, a "library-style tool" category) is a draft only.

## Why

M3-FINISH item 7, fix PR FIX-6 of the S3-MF-Audit report (PR #104, `S3-MF-Audit-findings.md` section 2). A-8 and A-9 were still open from S3-M3-Resolver-Review n1 and n3. I checked each finding in the code at origin/main 8a9dcb5 before fixing it, and all four were accurate.

## Changes

- **A-6 (code)**: the four internal engine-error exits in `jread` / `jlist` / `jtype` / `jsub` (bash) printed to stderr only. They now go through a new `die_internal` helper, which logs at detail first. The `snapshot` failure exits are also logged at detail now (bash: missing, invalid, engine failure; PS: missing, invalid). The stderr text is unchanged.
- **A-7**: in PS `exception-set`, a failed temp write now calls `Remove-TempFile $tmp`, as bash (`remove_tmp`) and PS `migrate` already did.
- **A-8**: PS write failures in `exception-set`, `register` and `migrate` now use the new `Get-WriteErrorText`. It unwraps the `MethodInvocationException` ("Exception calling "WriteAllText" ...") and words the common cases the way the perl engine's `$!` does: `Permission denied`, `No such file or directory`. The two resolvers now print identical lines for these failures.
- **A-9**: `Set-JValue` now matches segments exactly, as `Get-JNode` and perl `setp` do (new `Find-JProperty`). An exact-match loop alone would still differ: PowerShell would throw on `Add-Member` while perl adds a sibling key. That sibling also makes the file unreadable by PowerShell's `ConvertFrom-Json` ("contains keys with different casing", checked with pwsh 7). So both engines now refuse a new segment that equals an existing key ignoring case, with one message: `set: '<seg>' differs only in case from existing key '<key>'`. This follows the rule `rename` already has. PS `exception-set` wraps its edit in try/catch, so this failure gives the same `result failed` / `error exception-set failed: ...` / exit 2 as bash, instead of the trap. The perl header documents the new error. `renp` uses the shared `kfold` and its behaviour is unchanged.
- **tests/profile/test-profile.sh**: 55 new assertions: `jr-set-case-*` (last, middle and top-level segment, Unicode), `jr-set-exact`, `a9-exc-case`, `a78-exc-ro`, `a8-mig-ro`, `a8-reg-ro`, `a8-reg-nodir`, `a6-snap-missing`, `a6-snap-invalid`. `case_fresh` gained `NORM_TMP=1`, which normalises the per-PID temp file names before the parity diff.

## Verification (macOS, isolated HOME and AITOOLS_LOG_DIR under the worktree scratch)

- `bash -n` under bash 5.3 and `/bin/bash` 3.2: aitools-profile.sh and test-profile.sh clean. `perl -c scripts/aitools-json.pl`: OK. pwsh `Parser::ParseFile` on aitools-profile.ps1 and aitools-json.ps1: clean.

## Follow-ups

- A commander decision on I-3 (the draft above).
- RELEASE_NOTES.md entry. Not written here, since sibling fix missions touch it too. Suggested: one M3-FINISH entry from the orchestrator.
- `bash scripts/build-deploy.sh` plus a dotprofile commit after the M3-FINISH PRs merge (pre-push step 7).
- **PR #98 review m1** (scope addition from S3-M3Finish, NOT done because it changes a consumer): the resolver uses one message, "profile.json is not v2 or has no profiles", for two different states. `setup-user-config.sh:204` / `.ps1:208` classify that error as "profile.json invalid" by matching the substring "is not v2". Splitting the message would therefore change setup-user-config's summary row and ACTION for the v2-without-`profiles` state, and setup-user-config is S3-MF-NoMachine's file. Suggested follow-up, after that PR merges, as one change: two messages, "profile.json is not v2 (version X)" and "profile.json has no profiles object", in `resolve_machine` and `migrate`, in both languages; setup-user-config matches both; tests added to test-profile and test-config.
- Behaviour note for reviewers: `setup-user-config.sh` / `.ps1` now get "merge failed" on a config.json key that differs only in case from a managed key (e.g. `MachineId`). Before, bash wrote a sibling and PS overwrote the key.

######## PR #110 (2026-10-27T04:04:19Z) Cursor: fan out user-level rules only and prune against the rules manifest
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/110  +1206/-164 files=10
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Cursor/S3-H2-Cursor-Review-reverify.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Cursor/S3-H2-Cursor-Review-verdict.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Cursor/S3-H2-Cursor-drafts/reference/tool-ops-cursor.md.edit.txt, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Cursor/S3-H2-Cursor-drafts/registries/tool-ops.json.edit.txt, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Cursor/S3-H2-Cursor-handback.md, scripts/setup-user-cursor.ps1, scripts/setup-user-cursor.sh, tests/rules-fanout-cursor/convert-rule.ps1, tests/rules-fanout-cursor/step4-driver.ps1, tests/rules-fanout-cursor/test-rules-fanout-cursor.sh

Depends on: #100 (stacked on `feature/harness-rules-manifest`; merge after #100).

## Summary

`setup-user-cursor.sh/.ps1` step 4 now fans out only user-level rules (manifest source `shared` or `dotprofile`) to `~/.cursor/rules`, and removes the `.mdc` of every `aitools` (project) or `retired` rule that the fan-out created. A user's own `.mdc` is never removed. Mission S3-H2-Cursor (HARNESS-2).

## Why

Commander decision 2026-10-22: Claude Code gets everything in `~/.aitools/rules`, Cursor gets user-level rules only (HARNESS Q1). The rules manifest from #100 (HARNESS Q2, approved) names each rule's source. Without this, the #87 fan-out loads aitools' project rules (17 always-apply) in every Cursor chat under `~`.

## Changes

- `scripts/setup-user-cursor.sh` / `.ps1`, step 4:
  - Reads `~/.aitools/rules/.aitools-rules-manifest` (`read_rules_manifest` / `Read-RulesManifest`). A valid line has a `*.md` name with no `/` or `\` and no `.bak.`, a known source, and is not a case-insensitive duplicate. Invalid lines are ignored with a warning.
  - Fans out only `shared` / `dotprofile` names (matched case-insensitively). A file in `~/.aitools/rules` not in the manifest is logged and not fanned out. A user-level name with no file is a warning.
  - **Recognising fan-out files:** the converter now writes an aitools-fanout marker as the line after the frontmatter: `<!-- aitools-fanout: generated by setup-user-cursor from ~/.aitools/rules/<name>; edit that rule, not this file -->` (`cursor_rule_is_fanout` / `Test-CursorFanoutRule`). A file from the #87 fan-out (no marker) counts as created by the fan-out when its content equals the conversion of the harness copy.
  - **Pruning:** for each `aitools` or `retired` name, a recognised `~/.cursor/rules/<stem>.mdc` is backed up (`<file>.bak.<ts>`, per file, not `backup_dir`) and removed. Each removal gets a `DETAIL|cursor rules|removed: <file>` row. Any other file of that name is kept, with an info line saying so. If the name maps to a file deployed this run (a case-only difference), the file is not touched. Dry run logs only.
  - **No manifest, or a first line other than `# aitools rules manifest v1`:** nothing is fanned out and nothing is removed. The step writes a WARN row (`no rules manifest` / `rules manifest not v1`) and the ACTION "Run aitools to rebuild ~/.aitools/rules and its manifest". An unreadable manifest is an ERROR row.
  - Row when nothing is fanned out: `N removed` or `n/a (no user-level rules)`.
  - The manifest itself is skipped silently. It is a dot file: the `.sh` glob never lists it, and the `.ps1` skips names starting with `.` before any log. The #100 follow-up note ("Ignoring non-rule file ... .aitools-rules-manifest") does not reproduce on main's `setup-user-cursor.ps1` (the `StartsWith(".")` skip comes first), and a test now asserts that neither script mentions it.
  - PS1: a name containing `[ ] * ?` is backed up with `Copy-Item -LiteralPath`. `Backup-File` in aitools-lib uses wildcard paths and reports success without making a copy (not fixed here: aitools-lib is outside this mission).
- `tests/rules-fanout-cursor/`:
  - The existing cases get manifest fixtures.
  - New cases: users (only shared/dotprofile fanned out; a #87-era `aitools` `.mdc` and a marked `retired` `.mdc` removed with backups; user files with an aitools or retired name kept; `*.bak.*` and the manifest silent), rerun, nomanifest, badheader, invalid lines plus case-insensitive names, onlyremove, a `[ ]` name, dryprune, and a missing source.
  - New `step4-driver.ps1` runs step 4 of the `.ps1` on pwsh 7 (OS guard bypassed, isolated `USERPROFILE`). Every manifest case compares the `.ps1` result with the `.sh` result: exit code, `~/.cursor/rules` tree (names and md5), and summary rows.
  - `convert-rule.ps1` takes an optional rule name (marker parity).

## Verification (macOS, pwsh 7)

- `bash -n` on both `.sh` files. The pwsh parser is clean on `setup-user-cursor.ps1`, `step4-driver.ps1` and `convert-rule.ps1`. The only non-ASCII is the pre-existing em dash in the header lines 1-2.
- `bash tests/rules-fanout-cursor/test-rules-fanout-cursor.sh`: **164 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped** after the review fixes (it was 145 before them). The skipped case is dupcase, which needs a case-sensitive file system.
- Review: S3-H2-Cursor-Review found no blocking issues, with 4 findings (1 medium, 3 low). All 4 are fixed in 7f7fc5c and covered by the new cases edited, bodymark and lonecr, each with `.ps1` parity.
- Integration under an isolated HOME:
  - `deploy-harness.sh` (worktree sources plus a fixture dotprofile) wrote a manifest with 26 aitools, 2 shared and 1 dotprofile rules.
  - Seeded a #87-era `~/.cursor/rules` with all 29 converted rules, no marker.
  - First `setup-user-cursor.sh` run: 26 removed (backups kept); `hook-lifecycle.mdc`, `scratch.mdc` and `mine-dot.mdc` updated (marker added; non-interactive overwrite, so the row is WARN `3 updated`).
  - Rerun: `OK|cursor rules|3 verified`.
- MDM: `build-deploy.sh` under an isolated HOME with `AITOOLS_USER_REPO_PATH` set to a fixture dotprofile. The generated `deploy/setup-user-cursor.sh/.ps1` carry step 4, and `bash -n` passes. Run on the same #87-era fixture, the generated `.sh` gives the same result (26 removed, 3 kept). **No build-deploy.sh change is needed**: it extracts the header span (`# Does N things:` to the body marker) and the cursor body whole.
- `check-pre-commit.sh`: 1 FAIL, the known step 6 (harvesting file 100644). WARN on steps 3, 5, 7, 9, 11 and 13. Step 3 `--fix` was not run because it runs build-deploy against the real HOME.
- `check-pre-push.sh`: 1 FAIL, the known step 7 (stale dotprofile `deploy/`). WARN on steps 1, 5 and 6.

## Not run

- The `.ps1` on Windows. On macOS it was exercised only through `step4-driver.ps1` and the converter helper.
- Cursor itself loading a marker-bearing `.mdc` (the CLI is not authenticated). The marker is a markdown HTML comment in the body, after the frontmatter Cursor parses, and the loader takes the body as text (S3-H-Cursor-research B4).
- Interactive review: on the first run after this PR, each user-level rule shows one diff (the marker line) in the review menu.
- dupcase on Linux.

## Protected-file drafts awaiting approval

In `.aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Cursor/S3-H2-Cursor-drafts/` (both targets are added by open PR #99):

## Follow-ups

- aitools-lib `Backup-File` (PS1) uses `-Path` (wildcards), so a name with `[ ]` gets no backup but `BackupFileOk` stays true. `Deploy-ManagedFile` relies on it too. Library owner.
- After review (commit 7f7fc5c), a `.mdc` without the marker is also matched against the conversion of each `<name>.bak.*` backup that deploy-harness leaves beside the harness copy. So a rule edited or retired since the #87 run is still recognised. A file that still matches nothing is kept, with a warning and one ACTION row listing every kept file, because Cursor still loads it. This gap remains when the matching backup has been pruned (backup_file keeps 20).
- No `removed` outcome in the deploy tracker, so removals show as DETAIL rows and in the log line, not in the row text, when rules were also fanned out.

######## PR #111 (2026-10-27T04:24:17Z) MDM deploy-harness: payload data file, no-bootstrap skip, body markers, --skip-config
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/111  +1617/-182 files=12
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-MDM/S3-H2-MDM-drafts/dot-claude/rules/deploy-paths.md.edit.txt, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-MDM/S3-H2-MDM-drafts/reference/harness.md.edit.txt, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-MDM/S3-H2-MDM-handback.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/dot-claude/rules/managed-file-deployment.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/reference/harness.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-drafts/reference/managed-file-deployment.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-H2-Manifest/S3-H2-Manifest-handback.md, scripts/build-deploy.sh, scripts/deploy-harness.ps1, scripts/deploy-harness.sh, tests/deploy-harness-mdm/test-deploy-harness-mdm.sh, tests/deploy-harness/test-deploy-harness.sh

Depends on: #100 (stacked on `feature/harness-rules-manifest`; merge after #100).

## Summary

The generated MDM deploy-harness (`<dotprofile>/deploy/deploy-harness.sh/.ps1`) now works on a fresh endpoint, fits the Intune script limits, and is extracted by body markers. Mission S3-H2-MDM (HARNESS-2), approved HARNESS Q3, Q4 and Q6, plus `--skip-config` (S3-Harness2).

- **Q3**: when `~/.aitools/config.json` has no bootstrap keys (`reposPath`, `repoPath`), which means `aitools-install` has not run, the MDM variant does not run the config writer. It reports `OK|config.json|n/a (no bootstrap)` and exits 0. Before, this run exited 1 with `config.json not generated`.
- **`--skip-config` / `-SkipConfig`** (dev and MDM) uses the same code path, with the row `OK|config.json|n/a (written by installer)`. It is for installer Step 8b, which today runs the writer a second time.
- **Q4**: the harness snapshot moves out of the scripts into `deploy/deploy-harness.payload`. In a fixture build the scripts went from 2,279,683 / 2,309,986 B (.sh / .ps1) to 176,200 / 190,093 B. The payload is 2,075,245 B.
- **Q6**: markers of the form `# --- BEGIN/END <name> (extracted|replaced by build-deploy) ---` replace the section-heading cut points.

## Why

- **HARNESS Q3, Q4, Q6:** approved by the commander on 2026-10-22.
- **Q4 limits**, read on 2026-10-22 with chrome-devtools:
  - Intune, Windows PowerShell scripts (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/intune/device-management/tools/run-powershell-scripts-windows, page updated 2026-07-01): "The script must be less than 200 KB (ASCII)."
  - Intune, macOS shell scripts (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/intune/device-management/tools/run-shell-scripts-macos, page updated 2026-07-01): "The script file must be less than 1 MB in size."
  - Jamf Pro Scripts page (https://learn.jamf.com/r/en-US/jamf-pro-documentation-current/Scripts, version 11.33.0, last edition Apr 2, 2024): it states no script size limit, so the Jamf limit is unknown. The page only says policy logs are truncated at 25 KB.
  - The inline scripts (about 2.2 MB each) were over both Intune limits.
- **`--skip-config`:** `aitools install` writes config.json at Step 5, then again inside deploy-harness at Step 8b (#106).

## Changes

- **`scripts/deploy-harness.sh` / `.ps1`** (markers and the config skip path only; section 5b stays inside the extracted span):
  - Markers: `scope` (header), `body` (all code), `config bootstrap check` (section 1; a comment on the dev path) and `dotprofile lookup` (section 2). `--help` / `-Help` hide them.
  - `--skip-config` / `-SkipConfig`. Section 1 has one skip branch, used for the flag and for the MDM no-bootstrap case.
  - .ps1: the reason variable is named `$configSkip`. PowerShell names are case-insensitive, so `$skipConfig` would be the `[switch]` parameter itself; the tests caught that.
- **`scripts/build-deploy.sh`:**
  - `extract_markers` / `extract_marked`: each marker must be on exactly one line, in order, or the build fails.
  - The generated script is assembled from these parts, in order:
    1. body to the bootstrap check
    2. the MDM bootstrap check
    3. section 1
    4. MDM section 2
    5. sections 3-7 with the adopt target `""`
  - Payload file `deploy-harness.payload` (ASCII, LF):
    - a header line, then per file a record line `<path>\t<exec>\t<size>\t<sha256>`, the base64 lines and `--- end`;
    - the scripts embed its sha256, computed with CRs removed so a CRLF checkout still verifies;
    - one perl call unpacks it (.sh), or one loop (.ps1).
  - Failure outcomes:
    - payload missing: `ERROR payload missing`, exit 1;
    - whole-file sha256 mismatch: `ERROR payload damaged`, exit 1, nothing deployed;
    - a record with the wrong size or sha256 (kept as a second layer): `N embedded file(s) damaged`.
  - Small fix: the snapshot's `trap 'rm -rf "$MDM_TMP"' EXIT` replaced the BUILD_TMP trap, so BUILD_TMP leaked on every build. MDM_TMP now lives under BUILD_TMP.
- **`tests/deploy-harness-mdm/test-deploy-harness-mdm.sh`:** went from 30 to 57 cases. The new cases:
  - markers: no marker line copied; sections 1, 3 and 5b present; section 2 replaced. A missing END marker in the .sh and a duplicated BEGIN marker in the .ps1 each fail the build, and the dotprofile `deploy/` is left intact.
  - payload: present, not inline, its sha256 equals the script's; the scripts are under 200 KB (.ps1) and 1 MB (.sh).
  - config.json on MDM:
    - a fresh HOME exits 0 with the n/a row, the stub writer is not called and the files deploy;
    - with only `reposPath`, the result is the same n/a row;
    - with bootstrap keys, the writer runs.
  - config.json on dev: the dev path calls the writer; `--skip-config` works on dev and MDM, with config.json left untouched.
  - `--help` documents the flag.
  - damage: a damaged payload (checksum), a damaged record (sha patched), a CRLF payload (verifies), a missing payload, a truncated script.
  - .ps1: parse; fresh HOME; `-SkipConfig` on dev and MDM; `-Help`; CRLF, damaged and record cases; missing payload (temp directory removed).
  - `KEEP_TMP=1` keeps the temp directory for debugging.

## Review fixes (fdb5109)

The independent review (S3-H2-MDM-Review, https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/111#pullrequestreview-5623396152) returned BLOCKING with one finding, B1.

- **B1 (blocking).** With the payload moved out, the .sh truncation guard sat 85% into the script. A cut before it ran with exit 0 and deployed nothing, or failed to parse with exit 2, which reads as a user abort. I had moved the test's cut to 95% to fit, instead of moving the guard. Fix: the guard is now the first command of the generated .sh, before the inlined library. New tests cut at 40%, at the section 1 heading and at 95%.
- **N1.** Both readers now reject a malformed exec, size or sha field: `payload damaged`, exit 1. Before, the .ps1 exited 0 here.
- **N2.** The .ps1 path check now also rejects backslash and drive paths, and resolves the path to check it stays in the stage directory.
- **N3.** The build fails when deploy-harness.ps1 is 200,000 B or more, or deploy-harness.sh is 1,000,000 B or more. It warns within 5% of either limit. The deploy-harness.ps1 header is now ASCII.
- **N4.** The .ps1 cleanup check is now real: the damaged-record case runs with its own TMPDIR.
- **N5.1.** The .ps1 hashes the raw payload bytes, like the .sh.
- **N5.3.** Added the .ps1 "only reposPath" case.
- **N5.2 is not changed.** A corrupt config.json is handled differently by the .sh and .ps1 bootstrap checks. See Follow-ups.

######## PR #112 (2026-10-27T03:11:58Z) Fix setup-user-claude hostname/dry-run rows and build-deploy embed cleanup, plain build output (A-19, A-20, A-25, A-26 claude)
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/112  +603/-30 files=7
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Fix5/S3-MF-Fix5-Review-pr112.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Fix5/S3-MF-Fix5-report.md, .github/workflows/check.yml, scripts/build-deploy.sh, scripts/setup-user-claude.ps1, scripts/setup-user-claude.sh, tests/claude-mdm/test-claude-mdm.sh

## Summary

Fixes S3-MF-Audit findings A-19, A-20, A-25 and the claude part of A-26 in `scripts/setup-user-claude.sh/.ps1` and `scripts/build-deploy.sh`. Adds a new test suite, `tests/claude-mdm/test-claude-mdm.sh`, and runs it in CI.

## Why

These come from S3-MF-Audit (PR #104), M3-FINISH item 7. I checked each finding in the code before fixing it. All four were confirmed and none was rejected:

- **A-25**: setup-user-claude.sh ran `scutil`/`uname` itself (old lines 93-109), and the .ps1 read `COMPUTERNAME` (old lines 171-175). The lib already exposes `AITLIB_MACHINE_HOSTNAME` / `$script:AitLibMachineHostname`. The resolver puts `hostname` in every resolve result, including rc 1.
- **A-26**: the dry-run branch (sh 216-238, ps1 230-254) wrote no summary row.
- **A-19**: `Remove-AitoolsEmbed` only ran before the exit footer. On macOS, the generated .ps1 OS-guard exit left `aitools-embed-*` in TMPDIR, and run in-process (`& script`) it left `AITOOLS_PROFILE_PS1` set. The new test reproduces both on the unfixed code. A probe showed pwsh 7 runs `finally` on `exit N` and keeps the exit code.
- **A-20**: blog_warn/blog_error always printed ANSI codes. The child resolver's console went to build-deploy's stderr, and the entry points append that stderr to deploy.log (`aitools:2055`, `aitools.ps1:2004-2009`). The child's own `logging_init` had already written those lines to deploy.log, so they appeared twice. The new test reproduces both.

## Changes

- **A-25** (`setup-user-claude.sh/.ps1`): the footer hostname is now `AITLIB_MACHINE_HOSTNAME` / `$script:AitLibMachineHostname`, or "unknown" when it is empty. The scripts' own hostname detection is removed, so there is one P-Q5 source.
- **A-26, claude part** (`setup-user-claude.sh/.ps1`): a dry run now writes one row: `OK|claude.md|dry-run: would create`, `OK|claude.md|verified` or `OK|claude.md|dry-run: would update`. This is the config writer's wording.
  - The comparison now works the way `deploy_managed_file` / `Deploy-ManagedFile` compares: trailing newlines removed, case-sensitive.
  - Before, the .ps1 compared `Get-Content -Raw` output, which keeps its trailing newline, using `-eq`. So an up-to-date CLAUDE.md read as "differs", and the new row would have been wrong.
  - An unreadable CLAUDE.md is now a log_warn / LogWarn in both languages.
- **A-19** (`build-deploy.sh`): the PowerShell embed block now ends with `try {`.
  - The three generated .ps1 files that embed the resolver (setup-user-claude, -cursor, -settings) end with `} finally { Remove-AitoolsEmbed }` after their exit footer.
  - `Remove-AitoolsEmbed` now runs only once (a flag). The call before the footer still counts a failed removal as a warning, and the call in `finally` does nothing after it.
  - A post-build check fails the build if a resolver-embedding .ps1 does not end with the finally line, or if another .ps1 has that line.
- **A-20** (`build-deploy.sh`):
  - blog_warn/blog_error use colour only when stderr is a terminal.
  - The standalone child resolver's console copy goes only to a terminal, otherwise to /dev/null. Its lines are already in deploy.log, and its stderr is kept.
  - The entry points are unchanged: they belong to S3-MF-Runner.
- **Port (deploy-paths.md)**: setup-user-claude is a pure extract in build-deploy, so the A-25/A-26 code reaches deploy/ through the build. I checked the generated output.
- **Tests**: new `tests/claude-mdm/test-claude-mdm.sh` with 32 checks:
  - A-25 and A-26 on the dev path, .sh and .ps1, with dry-run row parity between them.
  - The build-deploy port, A-19 static checks and the A-19 runtime on pwsh (OS-guard exit, in-process run, full run).
  - Smoke runs of the generated MDM .sh.
  - A-20 with stderr not a terminal, and on a pty.
  - The resolver hostname is stubbed through the lib's `AITOOLS_PROFILE_SH` / `AITOOLS_PROFILE_PS1` override.
  - CI (`.github/workflows/check.yml`) runs it on macOS (bash 5 and /bin/bash 3.2) and Linux.

## Merge order

######## PR #113 (2026-10-27T02:39:48Z) Lib profile runner accepts result lines; user init uses it and passes --no-machine
https://github.com/nobul-tech/aitools/pull/113  +611/-123 files=9
FILES: .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Runner/S3-MF-Runner-Review-pr113.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Runner/S3-MF-Runner-plan.md, .aitools/channel/handoffs/2026-10-21_10cf255a-c-missions/S3-MF-Runner/S3-MF-Runner-report.md, scripts/aitools, scripts/aitools-lib.ps1, scripts/aitools-lib.sh, scripts/aitools.ps1, tests/profile/test-lib.sh, tests/profile/test-user-init.sh

## Summary

- The lib profile runner (`_aitlib_profile_run` / `Invoke-AitLibProfile`) accepts resolver output with a `result` line and no `via` line (what `register` and `migrate` print), and a bare value for `hostname` / `machine-id`.
- `aitools user init` (scripts/aitools, aitools.ps1) drops its private runners and uses the lib runner.
- User init passes `--no-machine` / `-NoMachine` to setup-user-config when it has concluded that this machine is not registered, so the writer adds no second machine-profile ERROR / ACTION.

## Why

M3-FINISH items 2 and 3 (commander): "The lib runner accepts 'result' lines (register/migrate print no 'via' line) so user init drops its private runner." The private runners existed only because the lib runner rewrote every exit 0/1 without a `via` line to 2. Item 2 consumes the writer flag from #98.

## Changes

Commit 1, e427e60 (lib + test-lib):
- Guard: exit 0/1 is trusted with a `via` or `result` line; for `hostname` / `machine-id` with a non-empty value. Anything else is still rewritten to 2 with an `error` line.
- A missing resolver: the runner still returns 1, and now also sets RC 2 with an `error` line naming it (the private runners' behaviour). Existing wrappers log their own message as before.
- A throw: RC 2 with an `error` line `aitools-profile CMD threw: MSG` (in-process PS, and the Git Bash pwsh branch).
- Git Bash branch: absolute values after `--profile`, `--out`, `--config` are converted with `cygpath -w`, as well as `--path` (what the private bash runner converted).
- The runner's temp-file removal is checked (`_aitlib_rm_tmp`, failure = detail line): the runner part of audit A-4.

Commit 2, 8967660 (user init + test-user-init):
- `ui_profile_run` / `Invoke-UiProfile` call the lib runner and copy its results (lib result-global contract); call sites unchanged.
- Deliberate differences from the private runners (review m1, m2): a failed temp-file removal is now a detail line, as in the lib, not a warning (a warning would promote every lib caller's OK rows, D-TS12); `AITOOLS_PROFILE_SH` / `AITOOLS_PROFILE_PS1` now override user init's resolver too, as for every lib caller (documented in both wrapper comments, commit 3).
- Flag: decided in the `machine_key` empty block after registration (scripts/aitools, aitools.ps1), not from an empty key at the writer call. The owner-"config" branch (registered, id undetectable) also empties the key and does not get the flag. The flag and `--machine-id` are never both passed.

## Contract (lib runner's accepted outputs)

| command | trusted when | else |
|---|---|---|
| `hostname`, `machine-id` | exit 0 and a non-empty value | rewritten to exit 2 + `error` line |
| every other command | exit 0 or 1 and a `via` or a `result` line (user-repo-path, resolve, exception-get, derive-config: `via`; register, migrate: `result`; exception-set: both) | rewritten to exit 2 + `error` line |
| any | exit 2, 3, 4 | passed through with the resolver's lines |
| resolver missing | runner returns 1, RC 2, `error` line naming it | |
| throw (PS) | RC 2, `error` line `aitools-profile CMD threw: MSG` | |

Case-by-case comparison with the removed private runners: S3-MF-Runner-plan.md section 1 (in this PR).

## Merge order

Merge after #103 (fix/mf-dotprofile-adopt-push) and #98 (feature/mf-config-no-machine). This branch merges both heads (945deb5, fae7d62); the diff against main includes them until they land.

**Merge order: after #98 and #108.** This branch is origin/main + `--no-ff` merges of `feature/mf-config-no-machine` (#98) and `fix/mf-audit-profile-json` (#108); the diff against main includes theirs until they land.

- A-10 (medium): on a failed derivation the writer wrote `ERROR|profile.json|invalid (see log)` + ACTION, or `ERROR|machine profile|no machine match` + ACTION, with no log line carrying either instruction (setup-user-config.sh:273-279 / ps1:266-274 on this base).

## Changes

| id | change |
|---|---|
| A-10 | `setup-user-config.sh/.ps1`: each section-1 ACTION is paired with one `log_error` / `LogError` naming the row and carrying the instruction: `config: profile.json invalid -- Fix profile.json in the dotprofile (details in deploy.log)`, `config: machine profile: no machine match -- Run: aitools user init (profiles: ...)`. Rows unchanged. |

## Assumptions

- **ACTION pairing level (I-2 / #147 not decided):** each section-1 ACTION accompanies an ERROR row, so its log line is `log_error` / `LogError`. This counts one more error on those paths (exit was already 1; the footer says "FAILED with N+1 error(s)"). Not `log_warn`: that would count a warning and auto-promote later OK rows. Section 3 keeps its existing `log_warn` pairing (its row is OK/WARN).
- **No version value in "is not v2":** I tried `(version X)`. A probe of 14 values showed the two engines print some numbers differently (`1e2` -> `1e2` vs `100`, `-0` vs `0`), which would add a parity gap. So the message carries no value.
- **A v2 file without a profiles object stays "invalid"**, with or without `--no-machine`, as the launch prompt and #98 say. `register` would repair it (it writes `profiles: {}` when the key is not an object), so "Run: aitools user init" is defensible advice; see Follow-ups.
- **Case twin: no ACTION row.** The error line names the key. An ACTION could co-occur with a section-1 ACTION and break "at most one ACTION".

## Verification

## Why

A child that died before its own `write_summary` (a `set -e` abort, exit 127, a pwsh parse error) disappeared from the end-of-run panel (script-standards-detail "Summary coverage rule").
- **A-2 (low):** `resolve_machine_profile` / `Resolve-MachineProfile` "resolver missing" wrote ERROR without an ACTION; every other failure path of the four wrappers writes one.
- **A-4, rest (low):** `rm -f "$rcf"` in `run_child_relayed` was unchecked. The profile-runner part shipped in #113 (`_aitlib_rm_tmp`).

## Changes

**A-3: `scripts/aitools-lib.sh` / `.ps1`, relay section**
- `run_child_relayed [--summary-tool TOOL] LABEL CMD [ARGS]`. The new optional leading flag matches `Invoke-ChildRelayed`'s 4th parameter. Calls without it behave as before.
- With a tool, a failed child that wrote **no ERROR row of its own** gets one. The rows, by case:

  | Case | Row (bash and PS) |
  |---|---|
  | non-zero exit | `ERROR\|TOOL\|script failed (exit N)` |
  | exit 2 | `aborted (exit 2)` (D-TS13) |
  | error lines with exit 0 | `logged errors (exit 0)` |
  | no command | `not run (no command)` |
  | mktemp failed (bash) | `not run (mktemp failed)` |
  | throw or parse error (PS) | `script failed (exception)` |

- The relay counts the ERROR rows in `AITOOLS_SUMMARY_FILE` before and after the child. Helpers: `_aitlib_summary_errors` / `Get-AitLibSummaryErrorCount` and `_aitlib_child_row` / `Write-AitLibChildRow`. An unreadable summary counts as 0 and is logged as a detail line.
- PS: an empty SCRIPT is now `not run (no command given)`, as in bash. Before, it was a throw with the "exception" row.
- Counters are unchanged: one `log_error` / `LogError` for a failed child; child error lines are still not added to ERRORS.

**A-2: same files, wrapper section**
- `resolve_machine_profile` / `Resolve-MachineProfile` "resolver missing" now writes ACTION `see deploy.log -- the dotprofile could not be resolved`. The text is the dotprofile wrapper's, so a run with both wrappers failing gets one ACTION (`_aitlib_action_once`).

**A-4 (rest): bash lib**
- `run_child_relayed` removes its exit-code file with `_aitlib_rm_tmp FILE LABEL`. A failed removal is a detail line naming the step; a warning would promote the parent's OK rows.
- `_aitlib_rm_tmp` takes an optional WHO (default `aitools-profile`, so #113's messages are unchanged).

**Tests: `tests/profile/test-lib.sh`**
- Every relay case now checks the summary rows.
- New relay cases: crash before any row (bash `false` under `set -e`, PS exit 1), exit 127, abort (exit 2), error lines with exit 0, PS throw, PS parse error, no summary tool, no command with a tool. A child that wrote its own row gets no second one.
- A-4 cases with `mktemp` stubs on PATH (macOS `mktemp` ignores TMPDIR): mktemp fails; the exit-code file cannot be removed (detail line, nothing else changes).
- A-2: `rmp-missing`, and `urp-rmp-missing` (one ACTION for both wrappers).

## Assumptions

- **Dedupe rule (design choice):** the relay adds a row only when the child wrote no ERROR row during its run, for any tool. Without it, every well-behaved failing child (log_error + ERROR row + exit 1) would show two ERROR rows. A-3's gap is the child that wrote none. If the commander wants "always one relay row", it is a one-line change in each helper.
- **Exit 2 detail is `aborted (exit 2)`**, not "failed", per D-TS13 (bare exit 2 = user abort). It is still an ERROR row and one counted error, as the installers count any non-zero exit today (reference/managed-file-deployment.md "[x]abort").
- **deploy.jsonl scope (I-1 / draft #162, undecided):** unchanged here. FIX-2 uses the audit's suggested answer.

## Merge order

1. #113 (`fix/mf-lib-runner-result`; it contains #103 and #98).
2. **This PR.**
3. FIX-2 (`fix/mf-installer-relay`, stacked on this branch).

## Verification (tested: macOS arm64; isolated HOME and AITOOLS_LOG_DIR under the worktree scratch)

- `bash -n` (GNU bash 5.3, /bin/bash 3.2.57) and the pwsh parser on `aitools-lib.sh`, `aitools-lib.ps1` and `test-lib.sh`: clean.

  | Suite | Before (#113) | After |
  |---|---|---|

  | test-user-init | 229/0, 128/0 | 229/0, 128/0 |
  | test-config | 269/0, 174/0 | 269/0, 174/0 |
  | test-profile | 689/0, 498/0 | 689/0, 498/0 |
  | tests/logging sh | | 24/0 (both) |
  | tests/logging ps | | 15/0 (5 skipped) |
  | managed-files sh / ps | | 57/0 / 39/0 |
  | adopt-push sh / ps | | 28/0 / 23/0 |
  | deploy-harness | | 45/0 |

- **Negative control:** the new test-lib against the pre-fix lib fails 37 assertions: every new row, the A-2 ACTION and the bash `--summary-tool` flag. It passes the PS throw and parse rows, which the old lib already wrote.
- **check-pre-commit:** step 6 FAIL (known: harvesting file mode); WARNs on steps 3, 5, 9, 11 (live dotprofile has uncommitted changes, not touched).
- **check-pre-push** (isolated HOME whose config.json points at the dotprofile; step 7 only `git archive`s it): step 7 FAIL is the known stale dotprofile `deploy/`. The fresh build's resolver consumers dry-ran clean: no "failed a dry run" in the step. Other steps PASS or advisory WARN.

## Review

The fixes are in 2ae798a:
